## 4 October 2026 — Basic even-force Ecaz Occupy composition

Two bounded owners supplied the source-band coalition quote and native Basic
runtime cases. One integration owner admitted Basic, migrated production
re-quotes, corrected a genuine total-dial validation gap and connected the CLI
help, canonical guide and source records.

The coalition quote now requires an explicit `advanced` boolean with no default
or alias. Active Basic requires an even Ecaz count and uses E/2 for the mandatory
contribution and ordinary-win Ecaz losses; Advanced keeps its ceiling arithmetic.
Canceled Basic permits the preserved odd count through the original own-pool
cancellation frame with zero fixed strength. The publisher page-8 paragraph and
page-15 example still contradict each other for odd counts, so an uncanceled odd
Basic battle is rejected at the original choice before a lead frame exists. It is
not rounded, canceled or replaced. Basic has no Emperor/Fremen elite inventory
(elites require Advanced or Homeworlds), and the engine normalizes a supplied
Basic support amount to zero rather than inventing paid Basic support; the shared
editor blocks nonzero support.

A genuine defect surfaced during integration: Basic `validatePlan` bounded the
dial by the ordinary own pool before the coalition quote ran, so a legal total
such as six was rejected. The quoted-force branch now includes the actual Occupy
profile, and the Basic Prescience numeric dial branch includes the coalition
lead; native feasibility still validates the exact total. The board projection
gains an optional public Basic-coalition flag so only fresh Basic tables collapse
the pair into one battle side; older played Basic games are not retrofitted.

The Basic fixture originally borrowed a wallet so the rival could afford its
eight-force sand shipment. Removing that artificial wallet exposed the real
unaffordable shipment; the fixture now transfers eight original rival reserves
to the battlefield after the genuine Nexus instead, keeping native wallets,
phases, alliance, plans and receipts. The genuine Basic fixture does not accept
an authenticated lobby yet; the authenticated staging path is separate.

Affected Basic/helper cases47/47 and the affected Advanced/CLI union352/352 on a
stable tree pass with types/lint. Five genuine Basic two-through-six-seat games
were run: four complete with 2,090 accepted actions, no rejections and 54 JSON
continuations; four-seat seed **20261299** is captured at the preserved
odd-count conflict after 321 accepted actions. The captured game, report, state
and trace remain outside the checkout and are not rerun or bypassed. The four
completed natural games contain no coalition lead choice, so the coalition
arithmetic evidence comes from the controlled programs and the human path, not
from those games.

Authenticated human390px **FEJ6SBFB v14** kept the admitted Ecaz/Guild/Emperor
seats and native wallets, then staged a conserved even-force board after the
genuine Nexus alliance. The table showed the correct Basic labels: a total-dial
slider bounded 2..6 (2 fixed Ecaz strength for four Ecaz forces plus Guild's
four-force variable pool) with no spice-support control. Real sealed plans —
Ecaz Sanya Ecaz 4 with total dial 4 against Emperor Hasimir Fenring 6 — produced
an 8–6 Ecaz win. The log and saved state record Ecaz's two mandatory Occupy
casualties, Guild's two variable casualties, and the Emperor's eight territory
forces to the Tanks; every seat remains at 20 physical forces after refresh.
The Basic Karama window, lead choice, plan editor and Collection continuation all
use the existing shared controls.

No natural-history claim for controlled positions, reset, public activation,
full-mode certification or deployed evidence; live **ab5c782** remains last
observed.

## 3 October 2026 — mixed current-fact/shipment Truthtrance

Two bounded owners supplied the public mixed tree/compiler/matcher (including
internal frozen-constant storage) and complete nested AND/OR authoring controls.
One integration owner owns game/truthtrance.ts, engine projection/promise
receipt, bots, the shared table guide and this checkpoint.

Current facts are evaluated once at the definite answer and stored privately as
three-valued constants; later legal changes cannot rewrite them, and unknown is
never collapsed to false. Future leaves still describe one eventual reserve
shipment or its absence under the existing FAQ whole-question truth. Multiple
ordinary and mixed promises constrain that same event together. Public
projection filters mixed promises to their respondent and keeps only the
original grouped question and whole answer in history.

reaction-order gap and public expansion gate remain.

## 29 September 2026 — owner account management prototype

The [account page](ADMIN_ACCOUNTS.md) lists only safe metadata to a live
owner. Provisioning uses a tab-scoped, browser-generated 256-bit key and an
exact durable operation receipt; only its SHA-256 hash enters the database.
Owners can change another account’s role or disable it after reviewing the
name, ID, current/desired role and reason. Database writes repeat live owner
authority and target-version checks, retain the final-owner trigger, and
attribute the single successful provision/role/disable audit event. Disabled
sessions fail immediately; room and seat rows remain untouched. Rotation,
re-enable, failed-attempt audit and complete administration remain pending.

An isolated direct D1 smoke provisioned a disposable account, changed its
role, disabled it, confirmed its old key/session fail and verified safe
owner-attributed history without a raw key. An isolated Request/Response
route-handler smoke confirmed owner provision, exact replay, directory read
and foreign-origin denial without contacting a persistent database. Focused
account/access/audit regressions include an additive migration preserving
existing room, seat, credential and audit bytes. The local browser showed
signed-out denial at phone width; intercepted owner fixtures displayed exact
owner-role disable confirmation, retained a one-time key after refresh and
hid directory/key controls after a focus-triggered demotion. Browser fixture
POSTs never reached an account database. Authenticated positive **network**
HTTP and live NAS acceptance remain untested.

Independent security review found and corrected unchanged-role audit no-ops
and incomplete final disable confirmation. A separate persistence review
reproduced a logout-all timestamp rollback; the account version is now
monotonic and a stale disable rejects. Both follow-up read-only reviews
reported no remaining scoped finding. The first focused HTTP run found a
test assuming JSON for Vite’s foreign-origin 403; the corrected boundary
test passes 3/3, and the full local HTTP suite passes 55/55.

`npm run check` passes typecheck, lint and 5,780/5,780 offline cases;
`npm run build` and `drizzle-kit check` pass. These results do not certify
complete administration, authenticated production mutations or deployment.

## 29 September 2026 — native Guild cross/return rate response

The classic Basic/Advanced Guild rate interruption now owns a typed, persisted
cross-planet/return declaration as well as ordinary reserve shipment. Guild,
its reciprocal ally and paid allied Fremen southern reserves can cross-ship
one exact physical group; only Guild returns an on-planet group to abstract
reserves. The prepayment Karama response binds original sector/elite groups,
turn, destination, quoted half-price cost and approved ally contribution.
Canceled rate uses full normal destination price for that same group; a Guild
return costs one spice per force under the explicit doubled-tariff
interpretation. If original resources or source custody cannot support it,
the declaration withdraws without payment, force transfer or consumed
shipment. The spent Karama keeps a replacement at full price for this turn.
Independent purchased shipping rates, Guild income and canceling transport
permission remain distinct; Homeworld and optional-module combinations are
not certified.

Focused engine/quote/in-memory SQLite cases cover group conservation, paid
Fremen transport, exact funding and unaffordable replacement, Basic/Advanced
responses, BG Worthless conversion, source corruption or depletion, private
views, legal AI candidates and a concurrent CAS race. A direct non-test
multi-sector smoke preserved 20 Guild spice and the source forces through
JSON reloading, then canceled the rate and settled three Carthag forces for
three spice with one Karama discarded. A local read-only Chromium seat-view
preview displayed the public prepayment rate and owner-only Cancel with
Karama button without persisting a room. These bounded cases do not certify
complete Guild, public Advanced or deployed behavior; see
[the selected transport policy](GUILD_RATE_KARAMA_RULES.md#native-transport-extension--29-september-2026).

A second read-only Chromium seat preview selected two physical forces after
an unaffordable Guild return; the replacement quoted **two spice** instead

Independent review found and verified a previously excluded affordable
four-force/two-spice Guild Nexus Secret Ally bot cross-shipment; its
independent card rate remains separate from the native rate. Existing
Moritani/Terror alliance continuations also required the classic-faction
roster gate rather than relying only on expansion flags. Eleven focused

## 29 September 2026 — bounded Guild rate Karama before payment

In classic-faction Basic/Advanced tables without optional modules, an ordinary

declared force cohort at full destination tariff without enlarging the
original approved ally contribution. If that split cannot fund full price,

declaration in the same turn uses full price. This conservative withdrawal is

cross-shipment, return and optional-module combinations remain gated.

saved/removed/archived/paused/closed rooms, active seat credentials, syntactically
invalid JSON saves, last room write, backup snapshot bytes/count and committed
private exports. Its read query repeats owner authority in SQL and returns no
saved state, seat token, message, backup payload or audit reason. The page is
read-only and refreshes only on request; it makes no integrity, disk-capacity,
repair or stalled-decision claim. Two isolated D1 tests pass for counts, live
demotion/revocation and unchanged saved games. The production HTTP suite
passed 54/54 with anonymous/forged-session denial, and the build includes the
new route/page. A real local signed-out browser denied access; desktop and
390-pixel samples rendered correctly using **mocked owner API responses** only,
not an authenticated production owner. Full administrator operations and
deployed acceptance remain gated; no production data was changed.

## 28 September 2026 — owner-only room backup capture

The new `/admin/backups` page lets an authenticated owner capture, list and
download an immutable private room snapshot. It includes the saved game and
related seat/recovery, lifecycle and discussion records without changing room
version or granting access. Exact operation replays, live database authorization,
content/version fencing and byte quotas protect competing writes and storage.
An append-only owner/room/time receipt records each successful private export
before its payload is returned. These are capture-only files: there is no import,
validated restore, server health display or unified action-history entry yet.

Twelve isolated in-memory D1 backup cases pass, including exact replay, revoked
access, unversioned concurrent changes, highly escaped state rejection, the
32,768-record download-audit capacity boundary and duplicate snapshots.
A throwaway genuine game created in an isolated store was
captured, listed and downloaded once; its room version remained zero and its
export receipt count was one. The signed-out local browser showed the owner
gate. A separate intercepted local UI view showed an older snapshot available
to download after its live room had disappeared; that view used mocked API
responses, not a real room restoration. The HTTP suite passed 53/53, including
a new anonymous, forged-admin and real room-host backup denial case; the build
included both new backup routes and the page. The stable full offline run
passed 5,662/5,662 cases with zero failures.
`npm run check` remains blocked by the unrelated concurrent Fremen Nexus draft's
missing `Player.fremenNexusMovementBlockedTurn` type field. The 1,800,000-byte
per-snapshot cap, 16 MiB per-room and 64 MiB global quotas, bounded export
ledger without archival, absent chunked backups, and unavailable live NAS Apps
session keep saved-game and deployed acceptance open. No production game or
credential was modified.

## Truthtrance current-spice checkpoint (2026-09-07 local date)

Truthtrance now asks whether another player currently holds exactly, at least or at most a nonnegative whole amount of personal spice. The server compares `p.spice` at the answer action, excluding separate pledged aid and incoming payments. It rejects dishonest/unknown answers to this knowable fact and publishes only the requested answer. AND/OR keep their three-valued semantics and reveal no additional per-clause results. The fact does not constrain later legal spending. Freeform interpretation, other facts and nonbattle promises remain incomplete.

The existing private-answer projection lets all four AI levels respond legally without new privileged information or balance fields. ## Fremen movement cancellation and Advanced setup checkpoint (2026-09-07 local date)

Ordinary Fremen movement now offers a Karama response only when the selected route uses its two-territory advantage. A stored turn/movement/group intent waits before any force moves. Allowance revalidates and continues the exact group through normal arrival handling. Cancellation spends one cancellation card, preserves the forces and unspent movement, and limits that particular movement use to one territory. Hajr or the second distinct Ornithopter group has a fresh range use; city and fixed-card ornithopter range remain independent. The November 2020 FAQ supersedes the April whole-phase answer; see [FREMEN_MOVEMENT_KARAMA_RULES.md](FREMEN_MOVEMENT_KARAMA_RULES.md).

The public response explains the destination and consequence. The mover sees the current restriction, and ordinary movement and normal-range Ornithopter controls reject an unavailable replacement route before submission. All four bot profiles use the projected current restriction; Hard and Brutal can cancel an enemy movement toward their own public fighters. Advanced Bene Gesserit setup bots now wait for the public Fremen placement prerequisite while completing their own traitor/prediction choices, then consider all legal printed starting sectors.

The complete existing run passed 1,431 tests, and all five subsequently registered independent movement edge tests passed on the same engine. The 116-test persisted/API suite passed. New verification consists of four BG setup tests, nine Fremen tests, five independent continuation/typed-custody tests and three production-room SQLite recovery/concurrency tests. ## AI battle legality checkpoint (2026-09-07 local date)

AI plans now require an available controlled leader or Cheap Hero, retaining the explicit no-leader and forbidden-Hero exceptions. Leaderless plans cannot carry battle cards or the Kwisatz Haderach. Voice compulsion uses each held card’s default role, while prohibition and actual usage use the selected battle slot. This preserves legal Chemistry/Weirding Way alternatives and canonical Portable Snooper behavior.

An own Prescience commitment now restricts the leader, weapon or defense candidates before scoring and slot validation. It no longer overwrites a validated plan afterward, so one physical card cannot be introduced into both slots and the final leader determines KH eligibility. Dial commitments retain typed casualty/support checks. Existing Truthtrance completion hints, Stone preflight, custody and hidden-information boundaries remain active. The authoritative engine and difficulty profiles were not changed.

AI battle selection now uses public fighter presence, storm order and an accessible same-territory path. Polar Sink, advisors, allies, earlier aggressors and storm-separated groups are excluded. The Hidden Mobile Stronghold uses its pointing sector for storm blocking. Concealed No-Field values are never consulted; public marker presence alone supports battle selection.

**1,418/1,418 registered rules/client/component tests and 113/113 persisted/API tests pass**, plus TypeScript, lint and production build. New coverage is three root Prescience cases, seven independently authored plan cases, nine independently authored battle-choice cases and three independent worm-ride regressions, each testing all four difficulties. Every candidate in those fixtures is applied through the authoritative engine, with private-view invariance checked separately. Final logs: `/tmp/dune-battle-ai-final-full.log`, `/tmp/dune-battle-ai-final-multiplayer.log`, `/tmp/dune-battle-ai-final2-type.log`, `/tmp/dune-battle-ai-final-lint.log`, `/tmp/dune-battle-ai-final-build.log`.

A broader player-count run exposed one Fremen worm ride aimed back at its origin. Ride candidates now exclude that territory and apply public destination-entry conditions before the target limit, while preserving unlimited ride distance, multiple source sectors, elite custody and the separate free-reinforcement rules. All original four-player accepted traces remain identical in the final reruns.

The original and second 96-game Basic calibration batches now complete **192/192 games with zero rejected candidates**, across 58,238 accepted actions. No deadlock, action-limit, exception or checked invariant failure occurred; every accepted action was the first offered candidate. All 96 original-seed accepted traces/final states match the preceding mobility run exactly, while 92/96 second-seed traces match. The second seed is a previously observed regression set, not a new blind strategy validation. Sources stayed stable. See [AI_BATTLE_CALIBRATION_20260907.md](AI_BATTLE_CALIBRATION_20260907.md) for preserved hashes, changed rows and non-monotonic difficulty outcomes.

The final separate two-through-six-player study completed **120/120 Basic games**, with 24 at each player count, 51,547 accepted actions, zero rejected candidates and no checked failures. Its only earlier rejection was the now-corrected worm ride. All recorded non-timing row fields match the initial study except that one rejection’s fields. The older harness does not record complete trace/state hashes or seed UUIDs, so no byte-identical replay is claimed. See [AI_PLAYER_COUNTS_20260907.md](AI_PLAYER_COUNTS_20260907.md). Combined with the final four-player pair, the current frozen code completed 312 games with zero attempted-candidate rejections; these different studies are not pooled as independent strength trials.

A live Basic two-player game also completed after the human Fremen enabled Brutal autopilot against the Hard Guild. The Guild won on turn 3 with four strongholds; a fresh invitation restored the completed v112 state and exact private seat. No battle occurred in that browser game, so its evidence is online progression, completion and recovery. See [VISUAL_PLAYTEST.md](VISUAL_PLAYTEST.md). The full Advanced/expansion scope and reliable difficulty ordering remain unfinished.

The independent [base Advanced readiness audit](BASE_ADVANCED_READINESS_20260907.md) separates concrete base-game gaps from unrelated expansion work. Immediate targets are the BG setup AI prerequisite, Fremen movement Karama cancellation, distinct Guild rate/transport cancellations, and unfinished Truthtrance obligations. Some special-Karama settlement policies still await authoritative resolution. Existing Advanced subsystem simulations bypass genuine Advanced setup, so authentic setup, complete games and browser recovery remain required before enabling that mode.

## Richese gift integration checkpoint (2026-09-06)

## Allied No-Field shipment and recovery checkpoint (2026-09-06)

- Added a persisted Richese offer-or-pass opportunity before the ally’s normal shipment. This preserves a human supplier’s decision when the active ally uses AI, regardless of seating order. The supplier privately selects a different token, destination and payment; the recipient accepts and selects its physical ordinary/elite mix or declines without cost. Denomination, typed offer details and recipient finances remain private to their entitled viewers.
- Successful shipment first reveals an existing supplier marker, immediately materializes the allied token from the recipient’s available reserves, updates shared no-repeat history and uses the recipient’s shipment slot. Prices are one spice in a stronghold or two elsewhere. Either ally can pay the full cost, or both can explicitly pay one for a two-spice shipment. Guild income and Heighliner accrual use the appropriate recipient/payment identities.
- Exact consent and token events fence stale/replayed actions. Richese cancellation and Guild prevention preserve token/fund custody; stale post-response allocation aborts safely. Arrival preflight catches unsupported simultaneous reactions before consent becomes committed, leaving decline available. AI tries legal acceptance and retains decline as a fallback after authoritative rejection.
- Fremen’s ordinary reserves are on-planet, so they do not satisfy this off-planet-only shipment method. Guild-recipient/active-Karama price and routing conflicts have been asked explicitly and remain guarded. ## Moritani allied card retention checkpoint (2026-09-06)

- A defeated Moritani ally may choose one eligible card from its revealed Battle Plan when the battle has a winner, or decline. The same mandatory-discard predicate serves winner cleanup and ally eligibility, including hero, Tooth, Artillery and traitor-preempted effects. Moritani itself and mutual-loss outcomes do not gain this benefit.
- The engine reserves the exact played cards as soon as combat settles. Winner casualty/card cleanup continues first, then ally retention, then the existing CHOAM income, technology, capture and Face Dancer continuation. Selecting a card declares the ordinary faction-power use with Moritani as Karama owner; cancellation discards all played cards, otherwise only the chosen card remains. No combat outcome, payment or RNG step is repeated.
- Controls use single-choice radios, full existing card rules and inspectors, an explicit decline and a visible declared-card inspector during the response. Reserved Bene Gesserit cards cannot be converted to Karama; reserved CHOAM cards cannot be cashed in. Public projection exposes only the previously revealed played cards and declaration, not the rest of the hand. All four AI profiles handle the choice and response.

## Terror entry, Robbery and Sabotage checkpoint

Persistent entry records now capture the entrant, location, committed force/elite counts, cause, turn/phase, effect stage and worm-ride continuation. Hooks cover ordinary shipment, cross-territory movement, Guild transport, spiritual-advisor arrival and worm rides. No-Field and Face Dancer modules remain outside this implementation. Self/allied entry and moving within the same territory do not open Terror. Existing occupation alone does not trigger it.

The Moritani owner may leave a token hidden or reveal an implemented effect. Robbery transfers half the entrant’s available spice rounded up, or draws before enforcing a chosen hand discard. Sabotage randomly discards once, then offers an optional own-card gift. Only authorized hands are projected; unrevealed identities and random discarded cards do not enter public logs. Reload preserves the stage and never replays shipment payment or the original force transfer. Truthtrance completion reconciles an already-cleared Robbery overflow. Fremen’s summoned-worm interruption now opens its owed Nexus before remaining rides after Terror completes.

## Current reaction and funding design

- `Game.decision` is a single server-gated decision union: advisor / wormProtection / wormRide / battleCards. `spiceResolution` persists resumable worm draws; `wormRides` resolves after all Nexus choices. Existing JSON rooms normalize missing fields on the next action.
- `Game.aid` holds spice earmarked by a donor for its ally in phase 3 or 5. A bid records its exact ally contribution. Unspent funding can be revoked unless committed to the current high bid; remaining funds return at phase end. Projections show only the player’s own pledge and ally credit.
- Harkonnen is an additional traitor voter when allied to a combatant, regardless of whether it holds a relevant card, preserving secret hand information. Resolution waits for its affirmative reveal or decline.
- Printed base PDF is downloaded at `/tmp/dune-rules/base.pdf`, extracted with layout at `/tmp/dune-rules/base.txt`. Reviewed pages 9–12, 16–19 confirm group movement, ally funding, optional discards and faction choices.

- `Battle.preparation` owns Voice/prescience steps. `prescience.value` commits a single element only after an existential legal-plan check, so Voice cannot force an impossible answer. `components/battle-preparation.tsx` owns these action forms. Karama cancellation now covers Voice and prescience. Add Truthtrance on top of these explicit windows.

## Official FAQ priority

The November 2020 official FAQ is indexed at https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Dune-FAQ-Nov-2020.pdf although direct fetch is still 403. Use targeted web searches to read its indexed sections. It supersedes the older base PDF mirror where they differ. Implemented: Weather Control and Family Atomics may be played after the dial distance is known but before actual movement. Remaining corrections: bribery is intended as advanced; Karama auction acquisition wording updated; audit simultaneous expansion stronghold victories. Storm-contested occupation is now excluded from victory counts. Truthtrance may ask any game-related yes/no question including current-turn commitments; unknown answers allow another question or later play. Full enforcement will need explicit structured commitments plus handling for freeform questions; do not silently limit its semantics and call it complete.

## Latest windows and cards

- `Game.stormPending` reveals the total before movement; all players confirm and card changes reset confirmations. Weather Control can replace revealed dials.
- `Game.spiceWindow` keeps each base spice blow open for Harvester before Nexus/charity. Harvester doubles new spice only; the old incorrect collection multiplier was removed.
- `Game.response` suspends normal play for a public power use. Every seat may allow; another faction may spend Karama to cancel. Supported: Voice, prescience (before answer), spiritual advisor, Emperor auction income, Harkonnen bonus card. More response kinds remain.
- Auction winner now receives an `auctionPayment` decision: pay bid or use Karama. Holding Karama allows overspice bids; an eligible player may take the current card directly. Full-hand limits still apply. Payment, income and bonus-card resolution are separated.
- Exact official Karama list was retrieved via targeted search `site:gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2020/11 "unlimited"`; indexed page 7 gives rules, superseding the April FAQ. Shipment with Karama costs Guild rates paid to the bank (not free); implemented with a benefit for the active recipient, who chooses their own shipment. Remaining base cancellations include Guild rates/transport, Fremen movement. Guild income and Harkonnen ally traitor support now have cancellation windows.
- Rule UI: `/rules` is searchable reference; `/rules/coverage` retains implementation ledger. Data in `game/reference.ts`; client in `components/rules-reference.tsx`; reusable `HelpTip` and TooltipProvider integrated in layout.

- AI: `game/bots.ts` accepts personalized views for candidate selection; authoritative rules filter candidates. `runBots` copies input state and advances up to 96 actions per transaction, with `botsPending` allowing authenticated continuation. Host-only lobby actions manage seats; no player cookie is issued for bots. `game/bot-profiles.ts` supplies UI descriptions without importing the engine into the client.
- Player-facing external hyperlinks have been removed from lobby and rule pages. Research provenance remains in developer documentation. Rule topic deep links open their target details.

- Fremen setup accepts sector-specific allocations and retains compatibility with earlier territory-level setup actions.
- Emperor alliance actions: immediately spendable spice gifts, and up to three extra allied force revivals at two spice each. Both have cancellation windows; canceled uses cost no spice or quota. Extra revivals are tracked separately from normal revival. Gift amounts are projected only to Emperor and recipient.
- Full base-faction reference topics now describe printed starting conditions, powers, allies and special victories. Six advanced-faction reference topics document their powers while clearly marking execution support as planned.

## Advanced combat checkpoint

- `game/combat.ts` enumerates casualty combinations matching both dial strength and spice support. Exhaustive independent small-army assignments verify the solver, including the printed Sardaukar example.
- Optional `Player.elites` tracks subsets of the existing total forces in reserves, tanks and individual sectors. Shipment, cross-shipment, multi-sector movement, worm rides, kills, revival and Ghola preserve elite counts. UI has explicit elite selection inputs.
- Advanced plans accept half-strength increments, seal support privately and reserve its spice against gifts/bribes. Prescience checks whether any legal support commitment permits the chosen dial without revealing that support.
- Battle resolution pays support (except a traitor winner), then gives the normal winner a `battleLosses` decision before card discards. Casualty options preserve the player's choice of token types; traitor/explosion losses include all elite tokens.
- Elite revival quota resets at the next turn, not when Revival starts. Ordinary revival, Ghola and Emperor extra revivals share it. November FAQ page 2 explicitly confirms no Ghola/Emperor/Tleilaxu exception.
- Advanced bot battle policies now evaluate feasible spice support and preserve valuable elites in casualty choices. Complete advanced AI games remain unverified because the remaining advanced rules are not implemented.
- Guild alliance transport now includes half-price off-planet shipment, on-planet cross-shipping, and Fremen paid cross-shipment from southern reserves. Only Guild may ship back to reserves. Allied cross-shipping pays the Guild, except the Guild-funded share, which pays the bank. Guild funding of its ally's ordinary shipment goes to the bank; only the recipient's paid share becomes Guild income.
- New primary evidence: November FAQ page 2 allows temporary allied-territory entry during shipping/movement provided the entrant moves out. Current `allowedEntry` still rejects allied destinations; must implement the complete movement obligation without creating unresolvable turns or inventing a voluntary-loss rule. Passing through an ally's territory is already possible.
- Latest full checkpoint: 141 unit/reference/combat/advanced tests + 3 persisted multiplayer tests, lint, typecheck and production build all pass. No deployment yet.

## Advanced storm/spice and foresight checkpoint

- Each advanced blow has its own discard pile and completes its Harvester/Nexus/ride sequence before the next blow. First-turn worms return after both blows. Additional worm placement is optional; its Karama cancellation blocks further placements for the current turn only.
- Fremen worm survival and optional allied protection have separate response windows before devouring. Only a surviving Fremen group can ride after the Nexus. Cancellation of one protection does not silently cancel the other.
- Advanced Fremen privately forecasts a randomly selected 1–6 storm card after movement. The next turn reveals that card instead of dialing; Weather Control can override it. Foresight cancellation hides the card without changing the distance. Without Fremen, normal dials remain.
- `stormResolution` resumes sector traversal around elite casualty decisions. Half losses round up per exposed group, and repeated laps recalculate against survivors. Legal Fremen reserve shipments into storm damage the arriving group only. Storm-protection responses cover the current storm movement or that individual shipment.
- Atreides auction and movement spice peeks now pause before private revelation. Each auction card gets a fresh response; a full Atreides hand does not suppress its peek. Movement shows one card even with double blow. An empty spice deck refills before foresight, preserving the revealed top card.
- Corrected allied Guild cross-shipment payments using the current GF9 base rulebook FAQ, page 23: all allied shipments pay Guild. The Guild's own contribution still pays the bank (November 2020 FAQ page 2). This supersedes the earlier implementation note saying cross-shipments pay the bank.
- End-of-game fallback is now implemented from base FAQ page 23: Fremen when Guild is absent; highest individual stronghold counts including ties when both are absent. Expansion victory interactions and optional extra-turn ties remain to implement.
- Advanced starts and all expansion starts remain disabled. These tests verify individual rules in advanced fixtures; they do not establish full advanced compliance.

## Guild timing and integrated checks

- `movementRemaining` tracks uncompleted turns independently of fixed storm order. Advanced Guild gets a now/wait choice at each boundary, may take its whole turn first/last/between others, and cannot interrupt an active player. Once Guild completes its turn it is removed from the queue permanently for that phase.
- The first attempt to take an early turn or delay the normal slot has a `guildTiming` Karama response. Cancellation locks normal order for that phase. Allowing a delayed turn grants the timing use; Guild can decide at subsequent boundaries without declaring its eventual position in advance. Flags reset at the next movement phase.
- Allied co-occupation resolution uses actual completed movement turns, which matters when Guild changes order. Temporary allied entry still needs implementation; the current entry check remains restrictive.
- Fixed a pre-existing recursive phase-transition bug: skipping an empty battle phase could invoke spice collection twice. The transition now returns after advancing; a regression test checks the exact per-force collection cap.
- Bot elite revival choices respect the shared one-per-turn limit, and stronger policies deliberately deploy elite reserves. Hard/Brutal scenarios combine the currently implemented advanced storm/spice/combat/timing systems and finish without stalling or violating elite conservation. This is not a claim of complete advanced play.
- Verified current checkpoint: 141 unit/AI/reference tests + 3 persisted multiplayer tests; typecheck, lint and production build pass. No deployment yet.
- Next advanced implementation: advisors and remaining cancellation/special Karama powers, followed by full timing audits. Then all expansion mechanics and full integration.
- Primary base page 13 explicitly resolves blow A and its Nexus before revealing blow B. Its combat example and increased spice-flow rule were retrieved directly from the indexed official GF9 PDF.

## KH and advanced economy checkpoint

- Advanced Collection adds 2 spice for Arrakeen, 2 for Carthag and 1 for Tuek's Sietch, stacking separately from ground-spice collection. Empty-battle transitions still pay collection only once.
- `Plan.kwisatz` is a privately sealed optional bonus. Validation requires advanced Atreides, seven accumulated battle losses, a living KH, a leader/Cheap Hero, matching per-turn territory and no cancellation. Loss counter/activation/used territory are projected only to Atreides. The battle's response window is identical whether KH is active or not.
- A surviving accompanying leader/hero adds +2; a killed one adds nothing. KH blocks traitor calls, including allied Harkonnen support through the shared voter path. A lasgun/shield explosion kills KH only if actually played and the explosion resolves; traitor victory overrides it. KH never increases normal leader bounty. Seventh-loss activation is immediate for another battle that turn; territorial use resets at the next turn.
- Normal KH revival costs 2 and shares the phase's one-leader revival quota. Eligibility tracks the required Atreides revival cycle independently; KH never participates in the five-leader cycle calculation. After revival, a subsequent KH death requires the next cycle. Ghola can return it early/free without using normal quota. The repeated/late-cycle interpretation still needs broader source and expansion-interaction audit before advanced mode is enabled.
- AI plans account for KH strength and traitor protection and can revive a dead KH. Advanced simulation fixtures remain partial rules integration, not full advanced-game certification.
- `components/revealed-battle.tsx` renders complete public plans for combatants and spectators, including card names, support and KH. The server supplies labels for played cards only after both plans reveal; unrelated hands remain hidden. Ghola now has an explicit force/leader/KH target selector.
- Primary evidence: GF9 base page 13 (stronghold income/combat), page 17 (KH/captures), November FAQ pages 3 and 7 (immediate activation and Karama), page 6 (Tleilaxu can offer KH revival). Official indexed GF9 source URLs remain above.
- Remaining timing audit: the guaranteed KH cancellation window currently precedes Voice/prescience. Broader legal card timing, including intervention after other preparation choices, still requires verification before full advanced compliance is claimed.

- Final verification for this checkpoint: 141 unit/AI/reference tests pass, plus the three persisted multiplayer journeys. TypeScript, lint and the production build pass. Advanced/expansion starts remain disabled; the full goal remains active.

## Harkonnen capture checkpoint

- Leader records remain with their original faction. `capturedBy` determines temporary control, and `controlledLeaders` is the common server source for battle validation, prescience feasibility and resolution. This avoids duplicating leaders or losing their death/revival history.
- A winning advanced Harkonnen battle queues capture after casualty and card choices. Harkonnen can decline; acceptance opens a Karama response before any random draw. Only surviving eligible leaders enter the draw, including the just-used leader in the same territory. Then Harkonnen privately chooses retention or execution.
- A `concealed` snapshot preserves outsiders' pre-capture knowledge without exposing the captive's identity through the original owner's leader roster. Captor and owner see actual state; Tleilaxu additionally sees concealed executions, not living captives. Revealed use or revival clears concealment. Turn resets clear obsolete territory markers from both current and concealed records.
- Execution pays 2 spice and places the leader face down, with at least two deaths. Live captives return after one battle; dead captives enter their original owner's tanks. Losing every native Harkonnen leader immediately returns unused captives. Captured leaders can turn traitor and retain their territory restrictions.
- Original-owner revival now treats capture as unavailability when opening a leader revival cycle. Executed face-down leaders remain restricted until their later cycle, with Ghola able to revive them early. KH cycle initialization avoids non-finite values when all native leaders are captured.
- Captive UI, ownership-aware leader selectors, AI capture/use/return policies and a searchable internal capture reference are implemented. Basic/advanced test suites include twelve capture scenarios and JSON round-trip privacy checks.
- Primary sources: GF9 base page 17, November 2020 FAQ pages 5–7, Ix Karama table. CHOAM FAQ page 12 says captured leaders provide only battle-use skill effects; leader skills and further expansion interactions remain to be implemented.

- Verified capture checkpoint: 141 unit/AI/reference tests and 3 persisted multiplayer tests pass. TypeScript, lint and production build pass. No deployment; advanced and expansion starts remain disabled while required rules are incomplete. The goal remains active.

## Combat cancellation and endgame checkpoint

- Advanced battle preparation now queues separate public response windows for elite strength and Fremen free spice support, alongside KH. Emperor gets no redundant elite-strength response against Fremen, where Sardaukar is already ordinary. No elite response is offered without elite forces in the battle.
- Canceling doubled strength changes combat arithmetic for that battle only; the physical elite token identity remains intact through casualties, reserves and revival. Canceling Fremen support requires spice for full strength, permits unsupported half strength, and retains the traitor-winner payment exemption. AI plans and player support forms use the same flags. Both cancellation notices are visible with the battle.

Full legal intervention timing after other preparation and before reveal remains a required audit: these guaranteed windows currently precede Voice/prescience. Advanced starts remain disabled.
- Victory counts exclude strongholds with opposing occupants, as specified by November FAQ page 1. Sole occupation under storm still counts. Final-turn fallback now awards Fremen when Guild is absent, or the highest individual qualifying stronghold count (all ties) when neither is present. Normal wins retain priority over special/fallback outcomes, and BG prediction cannot replace Guild/Fremen special wins.

The shared test setup now predicts a different seat when Bene Gesserit is the first player. The internal victory topic and Mentat guidance describe these cases without external links.

## Bene Gesserit advisor checkpoint

- `Player.advisors` records one stance per territory, with optional arrival-turn locks; existing force totals and sector counts stay canonical. `game/advisors.ts` distinguishes physical force counts from fighter counts, plans arrival stance and removes obsolete advisor records. Lone advisors promote automatically.
- Advisor presence no longer contributes to battles, normal/fallback stronghold victory, Fremen special-condition blocking, stronghold entry limits, ornithopters, collection/stronghold income or Atomics eligibility. Area destruction still includes advisors. Wrapping action results settles stance after a whole action, avoiding mid-explosion promotion. Collection, victory and battle enumeration settle before calculating their result.

Normal movement can preserve advisors, match existing fighters/advisors, or request an optional fighter flip after arrival. Arrival locks are carried when moving/joining advisors. Advanced starts remain disabled, so setup is exercised in controlled fixtures.

- Advanced free accompaniment offers valid sectors in the shipment territory as well as the Polar Sink. Successful advisor accompaniment locks that territory’s group for the turn. Before the first shipment, eligible advisor territories receive individual prepare-for-battle choices/responses; allies and storm-inaccessible battles are excluded.
- November FAQ page 4 supersedes April’s conflicting same-type/arrival text. Primary sources: base page 18 and November FAQ pages 4 and 7. Remaining audits include the full declaration timing range between Nexus and first shipment, mandatory/matching flips under Karama, precise scope of carried arrival locks, and all expansion interactions. Advanced charity and Worthless-as-Karama are still pending. Do not claim complete BG advanced compliance.

AI handles intrusion/declarations/accompaniment and uses fighter counts for enemy control/strength estimates. The internal advisor reference now documents these behaviors as Partial.
- The advisor storm test exposed a pre-existing graph bug: `distance(from, from, blocked)` returned zero before checking the blocked sector. Checking blockage first now prevents battles under storm even when both armies occupy the same location.
- Seventeen focused scenarios cover occupancy, economy, Atomics, movement/type matching, intrusion sequencing, setup, arrival locks, worm rides, storm/worm/explosion casualties, sector choices and JSON round-trip state. ## Bene Gesserit charity and Worthless Karama checkpoint

- `charityTurn` enforces one charity claim per turn for all factions, closing repeated claims after bribing proceeds away. Advanced BG receives +2 even from one spice and on turn one. At two or more spice the claim has a `bgCharity` response; cancellation consumes the claim without paying. The private view exposes claim availability only to the owner.
- `game/karama.ts` is the shared eligibility predicate used by engine, AI and player forms. Advanced BG can select Worthless cards for all currently implemented Karama uses, and holding one enables overspice bidding. Full-hand acquisition checks run before discarding. Sealed battle cards and prescience-committed cards cannot be spent again.
- `pendingKarama` stores a structured continuation for cancel/shipment/purchase/auctionPayment. A Worthless card is discarded before its `worthlessKarama` response. Allowed conversion completes the original use; canceled conversion grants no benefit and never returns the Worthless card. A canceled attempted cancellation restores the suspended response and its previous passes. The stored continuation is not projected, preventing private Emperor-gift amounts from leaking. Real Karama has no additional conversion window.
- Auction payment UI now selects the exact card. AI can claim advanced charity and use Worthless payment/cancellation. Internal searchable guidance covers the implemented behavior and remaining limitations.
- IMPORTANT unresolved source edge: the primary GF9 November FAQ confirms discarded Worthless cards on cancellation but does not specify an unfunded winning bid afterward. Current provisional recovery offers another funded payment or available Karama; otherwise it resets bidding for the same unseen card, original opener and unchanged ally funds. Do not treat the passing recovery test as proof of printed-rule compliance. Research surfaced similar restart rules in nonofficial compilations, which are NOT accepted as authoritative default rules. Confirm or replace this branch before enabling advanced mode.
- Primary sources: base rulebook page 18; November FAQ pages 7 and 9; Ix expansion Karama table. Advanced starts remain disabled. Twelve new focused scenarios cover charity thresholds/quota/cancellation, conversion timing, privacy and resumability, hand limits, bids/payments, commitment protection and private-view AI. All 184 unit/AI/reference tests and 3 persisted multiplayer journeys pass; typecheck, lint and production build pass. A final public-intent display check follows the same privacy test. The goal remains active, with no deployment.

## Emperor and Harkonnen special Karama checkpoint

- `specialKaramaUsed` persists once-per-game usage publicly per faction. The special card action validates advanced mode, phase, actual Karama ownership and unused power before applying effects. Ordinary Karama uses remain available afterward.
- Emperor’s Revival-phase power returns up to three own forces or one own dead leader for free, independently of normal quotas/cycle. The shared one-elite-per-turn limit applies and token totals are conserved. Living/captured/foreign leaders, combined force-plus-leader targets, wrong phases and ally-force targets are rejected atomically. Expansion revival/income and full timing audits remain.
- Harkonnen’s Bidding-phase power draws 1–4 unseen random cards from another hand, temporarily exceeding the hand limit if necessary. Its owner then returns exactly that many distinct cards from the combined hand, including any newly acquired ones. The card is discarded and game usage is consumed before the draw.
- `pendingExchange` privately suspends an existing auction decision/response. During the exchange all ordinary actions are gated. Completion restores the exact response including its passes and private amount, or resumes the pending payment. The continuation is never projected. Card IDs are absent from public exchange logs/decisions; each owner sees only their own current hand.
- Losing the only Karama backing a winning bid now uses the existing provisional unfunded-payment recovery. Auction finalization checks funding as well, so taking the backing card before the final pass cannot stall the game. This recovery remains a source-verification gap; tests prove conservation/resumability, not official printed-rule compliance.
- Player forms provide blind-draw amount/target, private return checkboxes, Emperor force/leader targets and help tips. Internal faction and special-Karama topics describe the two implemented powers and identify the remaining powers. AI selects targets from public hand counts and return cards from its own hand; Emperor can invoke special revival then continue normal revival.
- Twelve focused tests cover conservation, transient hand limits, privacy/JSON projection, suspension/resumption, atomic rejections, once-per-game usage, revival quotas and private-view AI. The AI invariant permits excess cards only for the active Harkonnen exchange, within its declared count.
- Primary evidence: GF9 base page 14; November FAQ page 3 (phase restrictions/once per game), page 6 (blind draw, inspect, equal return and temporary hand limits), page 2/base page 19 (one elite per turn). No tournament compilation was adopted as a primary rule source.
- All 196 unit/AI/reference tests and 3 persisted multiplayer journeys passed, along with typecheck, lint and production build. Final guidance-only validation follows. Advanced and expansion starts remain disabled; no deployment. The full goal remains active.

## Guild special shipment-stop checkpoint

- Ordinary off-planet shipments now validate destination, availability, elites, cost and private ally contribution before opening a Guild decision. The decision appears regardless of the Guild’s hidden hand until its once-per-game power is spent. Fremen southern reinforcements and on-planet cross/return transport are excluded; Guild’s own off-planet shipment can also be allowed/stopped under the rulebook’s “any one player” wording.
- `pendingShipment` stores the validated shipment privately. No payment, troop/elite placement, Guild income, intrusion or accompanying advisor happens during the pause. Only the Guild can allow it or spend its actual Karama to stop it. The special power resolves directly and cannot open a normal Karama counter-response. Mutation stays atomic and private projections never include pending funding.
- Allowing invokes the shared shipment commit once and resumes Guild income, then intrusion/advisor choices. Stopping spends Guild’s card and once-per-game power, consumes the shipper’s shipment opportunity, keeps troops/spice/allied credit untouched and leaves ordinary movement available. Any shipment-rate card is tracked privately and returned to its original owner on stop; allowed shipment consumes it normally. Converted Worthless benefits are tested as well.
- **Unverified settlement details:** GF9 base page 14 confirms one off-planet shipment of any one player; November FAQ page 3 supplies phase/once-per-game scope and page 7 establishes Fremen’s reserve immunity. Those retrieved primary passages do not settle retaining/returning rate cards, consuming the canceled shipment opportunity, stopping a separate spiritual advisor, or special-power counter timing. Current settlement is provisional engineering behavior, not a full-compliance claim. Historical/Avalon Hill/tournament and unofficial compilation text must not silently supersede GF9. Audit this before enabling advanced starts and extend it for homeworld/expansion interactions.
- UI provides declared target/count/sector, an allow button and a special-stop button only for actual cards in the Guild’s own hand, plus internal tooltips and rule links. AI at every difficulty resolves the decision from its private projection; higher difficulties protect occupied territories or contest substantial stronghold shipments and preserve allied shipments.
- Fourteen focused tests cover deferred effects, canceled and allowed payment accounting, elite conservation, allied contributions, gifted/converted rate cards, private projection, invalid/bypass attempts, once-per-game scope, movement after stop, basic/Fremen/cross-shipment exclusions, all AI difficulties and JSON-persisted intrusion continuation. Existing rate-benefit shape assertions now include the tracked card internally, while the public view preserves its prior shape.
- Primary sources: https://www.gf9games.com/dunegame/wp-content/uploads/Dune-Rulebook.pdf (p14); https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Dune-FAQ-Nov-2020.pdf (p3/p7). Product references remain internal only.

- Verified checkpoint: all 210 engine/AI/reference tests and all 3 persisted multiplayer tests pass, as do TypeScript, lint and production build. Advanced/expansion starts remain disabled. No deployment; the full goal remains active.

## Atreides full battle-plan inspection checkpoint

- Advanced Atreides now receives a special-Karama offer after Voice and ordinary prescience are resolved or declined. The offer occurs independently of its hidden hand and is available even in an unrelated battle. Declining preserves its card and game-wide usage; the offer cannot be reopened after sealing has begun.
- Spending an actual Karama consumes the once-per-game power and chooses either combatant. `Battle.fullPlan` records observer and target; the target must submit a fully validated plan first. Dial, leader, weapon, defense, spice support and KH are committed using the ordinary battle plan store, so existing support reservations and sealed-card protections apply. Invalid actions are atomic.
- Full-plan inspection now persists privately until public reveal without an acknowledgement decision. Its entitled Atreides owner can be an external ally; the assisted ally does not receive the private projection. Legacy saved `fullPlanRead` normalizes through authoritative recovery. Target-first sealing and the normal reveal/traitor sequence remain intact.
- A canceled ordinary prescience request still leads to the special offer. An answered element and the Voice remain binding during the full-plan submission. The special resolves without a normal cancellation window. Its game-wide usage persists across later battles/turns.
- Added a private plan panel and request/acknowledgement guidance. Existing battle form handles target commitment; other players see the specific wait. Internal faction, card, special-Karama and coverage topics include this behavior and identify incomplete timing coverage.
- AI can initiate, answer, decline and acknowledge the inspection at all four difficulties. When Atreides has inspected its own opponent, its battle scoring uses actual enemy dial/leader/weapon/defense, survivor strength and explosion risk. Unseen hand contents and traitors cannot alter the chosen candidates. Broader difficulty calibration still remains.
- Twelve dedicated scenarios cover Voice/prescience ordering, targeted commitment, selected-card privacy, acknowledgement ownership, unrelated battles/allies, hidden-hand-independent offers, ordinary cancellation, invalid input atomicity, once-per-game scope, sealed Worthless protection, all four AI difficulties, actual-information tactics and KH visibility. Existing advanced combat/KH/advisor fixtures now explicitly decline the new offer before testing their original mechanics; their original assertions are retained.
- Primary evidence: GF9 base rulebook p14 (once-per-game Karama; inspect any one player’s entire Battle Plan), November 2020 FAQ p3/p9 (advanced power scope/once-per-game). The exact post-prescience commitment/acknowledgement order is an engineering timing choice pending the remaining pre-reveal card audit. Re-answer/replanning rules after other card effects, interaction with cancellations already committed, and all expansion plan components must still be audited. Historical/unofficial compilations were not adopted as proof of GF9 compliance.
- Advanced and expansion table starts remain disabled; no deployment. The full goal remains active.

## Fremen summoned sandworm checkpoint

- Added once-per-game Fremen Karama during Spice Blow and Nexus, restricted to sand. The call does not draw/discard a spice card or mutate the existing discard-pile order. Destruction, optional ally protection, ordinary Karama cancellation of protection, elite conservation and worm rides reuse the existing worm engine.
- `summonedWorm.resume` privately suspends the interrupted response/decision, Worthless conversion, fresh-blow window and spice sequence until protection/destruction finishes. Only the called territory is projected. Resumption preserves response passes and private payloads; devoured fresh spice is marked unavailable to Harvester.

The initial local interruption prototype was replaced before release. A pre-draw call now defers its Nexus while preserving its ride queue; normal draw completion opens negotiations. In advanced play this follows the current pass’s ordinary Nexus point before proceeding to the other pile. Calling during an open Nexus adds the new ride and resets confirmations. A call during an unmade ride requeues it and opens negotiations; a completed ride’s pending BG intrusion/flip resolves before the new Nexus.

All four difficulty policies can initiate the call; existing response/Nexus/ride policies perform the continuation.

It supersedes the older contradictory “no Nexus” answer still printed in the current base PDF p22. Base p14 supplies sand placement/one-use scope. ## Tech-token module checkpoint

Ecaz & Moritani rulebook page 15 reiterates that allies cannot combine token holdings and says homeworld shipments trigger Heighliners (homeworld engine still unimplemented).

Each occupies exactly one slot.

## Poison Tooth and Artillery Strike checkpoint

- Added `poisonTooth` and `artillery` card identities to the implemented Ix battle-card catalog. Full room decks still use the gated base setup; no partial expansion deck is offered.
- Battle revelation now opens an owner-only Poison Tooth activation decision before traitor choices. This is separate from the sealed plan and ordinary prescience commitments. Activation is public, serialized and immutable once submitted. No leaders, forces or spice are changed until battle resolution. The queue can handle multiple tooth-bearing plans in storm order for future card-copy effects; complete Mirror Weapon semantics remain unimplemented.
- Shared `battleCardEffects` handles attacks on both leaders. A tooth can be declined, or activated against each side's defense; Chemistry as defense protects while Snooper/Shield Snooper do not. Unused winning tooth is optionally retained; used tooth is discarded. Artillery attacks each side's shield, suppresses surviving leader/KH strength and all leader bounty, and is discarded after use. Other weapons still apply, and advanced spice support/casualty accounting is preserved.
- Traitor victories bypass these effects and preserve the winning faction's otherwise single-use weapon; explosions take precedence. This follows the base rule that the successful traitor caller loses nothing. Opposing leaders/forces/cards follow normal traitor losses.
- AI models both leaders' exposure and artillery suppression during planning. At the revealed tooth decision it compares public totals with/without activation, preferring a win and preserving its leader and unused card when possible. Easy retains variation; Medium/Hard/Brutal choose based on these outcomes. The UI shows revealed plans to the tooth owner, activation/decline controls and the submitted result; internal card topics describe effects, timing and current coverage.
- Evidence: GF9 November 2020 FAQ p8 explicitly covers Chemistry versus tooth, Snooper exceptions, used/unused tooth discard and Shield Snooper explosions; p9 confirms Shield Snooper versus Artillery and the Weirding Way exception. Pages 4–5 govern named versus generic Voice. CHOAM & Richese rulebook p4 directs replacement of the original tooth/artillery cards. Direct retrieval of the replacement printed card faces remains incomplete, so do not claim that printing audit is finished; the implementation currently follows the FAQ-backed behavior and known card text. Do not use the separately found Dune movie-game rulebook as classic-game evidence. Stone Burner, leader skills, Mirror Weapon and other later card interactions still need implementation/audit; specifically confirm artillery/KH suppression against later clarifications.
- Verification: all 277 unit/engine/AI/reference tests and all four persisted multiplayer journeys pass. The 12 new tests cover ownership, invalid-action immutability, JSON/private revelation, activation, retained/discarded tooth, defenses on both sides, self-exposure, suppressed bounty, traitor/explosion precedence, Voice, four AI levels and advanced force/spice/KH outcomes. TypeScript, final lint and production build pass. Final coverage text was updated after build. No visual browser QA or deployment; full goal remains active.

## Sandtrout spice-deck checkpoint

- Added a Sandtrout spice-card variant and optional 22-card Ix deck construction. Start chooses that deck when the Ix expansion is selected, but the existing expansion-start gate still applies. The 21-card base deck remains unchanged.
- Drawing Sandtrout breaks alliances, clears offers and returns any outstanding ally credit to its donor. It is represented as a public set-aside card while waiting, so it cannot re-enter a reshuffle before consumption. Normal territory draws do not consume it, including between advanced piles and across turns.
- A subsequent active drawn Shai-Hulud consumes Sandtrout without destruction, protection responses, Fremen rides or its own Nexus. Only an immediate territory replacement receives double spice. A second worm resolves normally against the preceding effective card and cancels the bonus. Suppressed discard entries carry metadata so they do not count as normal worms for Fremen extra placement; reshuffling strips that metadata.
- Harvester doubles the enhanced amount; storm still prevents it. First-turn ignored worms currently do not consume a waiting Sandtrout. A Fremen summoned worm is not a drawn card and does not consume it. A prior already-triggered Nexus is preserved when Sandtrout appears later in that draw sequence.
- Added a public pending-status notice, an internal searchable Sandtrout topic, and the previously missing table display for Atreides' private spice foresight (territory, Shai-Hulud or Sandtrout). The existing server projection controls who can see the next card. Status notices sit outside the play grid so they do not displace its columns.
- Primary evidence: GF9 Ixians & Tleilaxu rulebook p4 (add one Sandtrout) and p11 (immediate alliance cancellation, next Shai-Hulud suppression, immediate land doubling or second-worm normal Nexus), at `https://www.gf9games.com/dunegame/wp-content/uploads/2020/09/IxianAndTleilaxuRulebook.pdf`. First-turn ordering, the exact suppressed-card discard layout, prior-worm Nexus and special-summon interpretations remain audit items; later discovery and other expansion spice interactions are not implemented. Live expansion starts stay gated pending full integration and that audit.
- Verification: all 292 engine/AI/reference tests and all four persisted multiplayer journeys pass. The 14 Sandtrout scenarios cover deck inventory, alliance/escrow cleanup, pending-state JSON, suppression versus normal worms, both advanced piles, Fremen protection/placement, Harvester/storm, reshuffle metadata and private foresight. An additional full-game AI test runs Hard/Brutal with Sandtrout and checks exactly one card across deck/discards/set-aside state plus force conservation. Final TypeScript, lint and production build all pass. No browser QA or deployment; full goal remains active.
