The reserve is stored authoritatively and never projected. Staged fixture setup covers conservation and secrecy; live expansion-start gates remain in place.
- Every other faction's battle victory opens an optional Tleilaxu decision independently of its hidden holdings, after winner losses/card choices/tech transfers/Harkonnen capture. A matching unrevealed Face Dancer returns the winner's surviving forces to reserves, preserving elite subsets, and allows replacement from Tleilaxu reserves and any board locations up to that count. Killing a living winning leader adds no bounty and does not repeat an earlier death. Cheap Hero identity survives its treachery discard for matching; KH cannot prevent Face Dancing. Karama cannot cancel it. Winner rewards remain unchanged.
- Replacement currently removes the winning faction's forces, preserves peaceful BG bystanders, permits zero replacement forces, and lets Tleilaxu choose a sector in the battle territory. These sector/zero/bystander readings and later mobile-stronghold/homeworld/terror/ambassador interactions remain explicit cross-module audit items. Captive release is rechecked when a Face Dancer kills Harkonnen's last native leader.
- Revealed Face Dancers are public and unavailable for reuse. Revealing the third shuffles all three into the reserve and deals three new cards. During Mentat, Tleilaxu may replace one unrevealed card with a cancelable response. The selected identity is hidden from other players in the response projection; cancellation preserves the cards and uses the turn's replacement opportunity. Full simultaneous timing and Mentat/victory ordering still require the global audit.
- `battleLeaderStrength` copies the opposing disc for Zoal, excluding KH, and uses zero against a hero. Ordinary totals, poison/weapon bounty and traitor bounty share the helper. Zoal retains a separate effective ordinary revival value of three through his catalog strength; battle selectors display X. AI sealed-plan estimation and Poison Tooth evaluation use dynamic leader strength.
- Added an owner-only Face Dancer control component with sources, sector and counts; private hand and public revealed-card panels; Mentat replacement buttons; searchable rule/faction entries. AI at all four levels can reveal/decline using its own private identities, preserves one force in source strongholds, and replaces dead-leader Face Dancers during Mentat. Tleilaxu revival strategy is not yet implemented.
- Closed a start-validation gap: an expansion faction supplied with an empty expansion selection is rejected, so direct API construction cannot silently start an unfinished faction. All expansion starts remain gated pending actual completion.
- Primary evidence: Ix printed p3, p6–7, p10–12; November 2020 FAQ p6 and p8 at `https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Dune-FAQ-Nov-2020.pdf`. The FAQ confirms unlimited Tleilaxu force/own-leader revival, their own free-revival income, Zoal revival value three/no copied KH bonus, Emperor extra-revival payments to Tleilaxu and the separate Karama effects. Revival income/discounts/negotiation, foreign gholas, special Karama and their UI/AI are the next major Tleilaxu work, not completed here.
- Nineteen focused scenarios pass: private setup/deck conservation, reward ordering, force/elite conservation, invalid-action immutability, declined/unmatched reveals, Cheap Hero, KH, dead leaders, third-card recycling, private/canceled Mentat replacement, Zoal totals/bounty, all AI difficulties, unfinished-faction gating, BG bystanders and Harkonnen captive release. The fixture's Guild-to-Tleilaxu substitution now remaps dealt identities before setup; an earlier randomized fixture failure was corrected. Final regression/build results are recorded below.

- Setup finalization is shared by all successful setup actions and bot continuations. This prevents the Harkonnen–Tleilaxu two-faction combination from waiting indefinitely when neither faction has an ordinary traitor-selection prompt. A nineteenth scenario covers automatic completion and deck conservation for that pairing.
- Final verification: all 347 engine/AI/reference tests and all four persisted multiplayer journeys pass. TypeScript, lint and production build pass after the setup-finalization and UI/reference edits. No browser visual QA or deployment. The original goal remains active and incomplete; the next required Tleilaxu work is revival economy/negotiation, foreign gholas and special Karama.

## Tleilaxu revival commerce checkpoint

- Added shared revival quoting and transactions in `game/revival.ts` and the authoritative engine. Tleilaxu can revive their own forces without the ordinary three-force limit, revive multiple own leaders before all five are dead, and pay half normal prices rounded up. Other factions retain their native revival-cycle and one-leader restrictions. Elite revival remains limited to one per turn across ordinary, Ghola and Emperor-funded revivals.
- Tleilaxu can grant another faction up to five normal force revivals and grant their current ally half-price ordinary revival. The recipient pays; no implicit Tleilaxu funding is created. Dedicated Karama responses occur when the extra limit or paid discount is used. A canceled limit aborts the transaction without partial revival; a canceled discount restores the normal price and aborts if the payer cannot afford it.
- Other factions privately request an early dead leader or KH, and Tleilaxu quotes or declines. Acceptance revalidates eligibility, funds and the one-leader allowance before its Karama response. A zero-spice agreement is allowed. When all native leaders are unavailable, including capture, ordinary revival rules apply instead of the early negotiation path. Early-revival cancellation blocks that selected leader for the phase without revealing a concealed identity to outsiders.
- Paid revivals now route to Tleilaxu through a separate income response. Normal free revival rewards one spice once per faction per turn, including Tleilaxu themselves; split free-force actions and canceled income cannot repeat that reward. A Ghola treachery play has its separate one-spice reward, including outside the Revival phase. Income cancellation preserves the completed revival. Emperor-funded extra allied forces also pay Tleilaxu, and their separate three-force quota can combine with a granted normal five for eight forces.
- Pending transactions serialize safely and remain internal. Quote records are visible only to Tleilaxu and the requester; payment amounts only to Tleilaxu and the payer. Target IDs are validated against actual players before indexing request records, including rejection of prototype-property targets. No hidden rival spice enters AI quotation or revival choices.
- Added `components/revival-commerce.tsx`, private quote/accept/decline/withdraw controls, Tleilaxu permission controls, remaining-limit/free-revival/price guidance, response labels and an internal searchable revival rule entry. All four AI levels can quote, grant permissions, request/accept eligible early returns, use discounts and complete the phase through response windows.
- Primary evidence: printed Ix rulebook p7 and November 2020 FAQ p6 and p8. The FAQ confirms unlimited own leaders, one leader for other factions, half-price rounding, KH negotiations, captured-leader unavailability, ally self-payment and Emperor extra-force income. Sources are the genuine publisher-rulebook mirror and official November FAQ recorded above. Foreign gholas, special Karama, Ix cyborg pricing and later expansion economy remain unimplemented. Exact cancellation duration/scope, combined paid/free-income cancellation, ally-permission timing, Emperor special free-revival income and full card-window integration remain audit items; tournament-wide free-income cancellation rules have not been silently adopted.
- Verification: all 368 engine/AI/reference tests and all four persisted multiplayer journeys pass. The 21 focused revival scenarios cover private quotes, rejection/immutability, zero-price and KH revival, captured/concealed leaders, repeated free-income prevention, canceled discounts/limits/payments, Zoal rounding, elite/tech-token accounting, Emperor funding and complete AI phase progression. Final TypeScript, lint and production build pass. No browser visual QA or deployment. The original goal remains active; expansion starts stay gated until their full rules and interactions are complete.

## Foreign ghola checkpoint

- Added `game/leader-control.ts` and persistent `Leader.gholaBy`. Every leader remains a single record under its original faction, preserving traitor identity and death history; ghola control overrides native use, and temporary Harkonnen capture overrides ghola control. The engine, battle preparation, battle selectors and AI share the controller predicate. Public ghola pools are projected without copying authoritative identities.
- Advanced Tleilaxu can acquire dead foreign leaders during Revival at their discounted bank price while their active controlled pool has fewer than five leaders. Multiple acquisitions are allowed up to that pool limit. Face-down executed leaders remain eligible and retain death counts. KH and the Auditor are excluded. The full-price discount cancellation still reprices before any payment or transfer.
- Acquisition has a dedicated Karama response. Cancellation aborts it without spice or leader mutation and blocks that particular foreign leader for the current turn; another eligible leader can be chosen. Selected pending identities and canceled-identity history remain private to Tleilaxu. The turn key persists independently of Revival-phase initialization.
- Foreign gholas remain associated with Tleilaxu after death. A living ghola cannot be sold back. The native faction may request a private dead-ghola buyback through the existing quote/decline/accept controls even when all its native leaders are unavailable. Acceptance restores native control and uses the one-leader revival allowance. Stale quotes are revalidated. Ordinary paid revival and Emperor special revival cannot bypass ghola ownership; the Ghola treachery card can revive an existing leader in the acting faction's controlled pool.
- A foreign ghola is unavailable to its native faction for ordinary cycle eligibility. It can fight for Tleilaxu, turn traitor under its original identity, die again and retain the death history. Capture of a foreign ghola now reveals the captured identity to its current controller and Harkonnen; the original faction is an outsider to that capture. Capture logs identify the losing controller. After one Harkonnen use, capture clears and Tleilaxu control resumes.
- Added foreign-ghola revival controls, price/limit checks, pool status, dead-ghola buyback labels and a searchable internal `tleilaxu-gholas` rule topic. All four AI difficulties select affordable gholas from their personalized projection, recognize combat control and request eligible buybacks when their native leaders are unavailable. No player-facing external links were added.
- Primary evidence: publisher Ix rulebook p7 (acquisition and five-active-leader pool), p10 (buyback permission) and p12 (Karama table); November 2020 official FAQ p6 supersedes the older living-ghola buyback wording, confirms multiple foreign revivals and face-down leader history. CHOAM rulebook p8 excludes the Auditor. Original sources and the genuine PDF mirror remain recorded above. Remaining audits include exact buyback pricing/normal-revival and card exceptions, captured-leader pool counting, returning own leaders above five total active leaders, Harkonnen native-ghola/captive-release edges, cancellation timing and all leader-skill/homeworld/Ecaz interactions. Tleilaxu special Karama remains unimplemented; expansion starts remain gated.
- Sixteen focused scenarios cover acquisition/payment/identity conservation, JSON/public pools, five-leader cap, authority/timing/funds, specific cancellation and privacy, discount cancellation, face-down leaders, living/stale/declined buybacks, original-owner cycles, Ghola card ownership, traitors, illegal native battle use, capture and return, all AI difficulties and complete AI revival progression. Final full verification passes all 384 engine/AI/reference tests, including the additional AI buyback-selection regression. All four persisted multiplayer journeys also pass. TypeScript, lint and the production build pass. No browser visual QA or deployment. The full goal remains active and incomplete.

## Tleilaxu special Karama checkpoint

- Added an authoritative `revivalStop` decision before another faction's advanced normal revival commits. The window appears independently of Tleilaxu's hidden card holdings while their once-per-game power is unused. Only Tleilaxu may allow or spend a real Karama. The decision exposes the recipient and force/leader category, not the pending identity, price or private continuation. A successful stop consumes the card once, marks the special power spent permanently and cancels the pending transaction without spice, force, leader, elite or ordinary-revival quota mutation.
- Current implementation prevents the selected faction's remaining normal force and leader revivals for that turn's Revival phase. It covers ordinary revivals, negotiated leaders, buybacks, KH and Emperor-funded allied extra forces. Requests for the stopped faction are cleared; later requests/revivals reject, while other factions and the next turn remain eligible. Already completed revivals are not undone. Ghola treachery and Emperor special Karama currently remain separate. No ordinary cancellation response is created for the special use itself.
- Emperor allied extra revivals now use the shared pending revival transaction after their ordinary Emperor-power response. The Emperor remains the payer; the ally receives forces and the separate extra quota; Tleilaxu receives paid-revival income only after completion. A stopped extra revival consumes neither the Emperor's spice nor the ally's force/elite/extra quota. Shared transactions carry explicit payer/source fields and serialize through JSON.
- Added a player decision panel, matching tooltip, public prevention status, blocked-revival guidance and updated internal faction/special-power/coverage references. All four AI difficulties allow without a Karama, protect allies and use public tank/leader threats to choose when to spend it. Blocked bots pass normal Revival and continue the game; the policy does not inspect rival spice or concealed leader identities.
- Primary source: printed publisher Ix rulebook p6 and indexed official PDF describe preventing a player's revival, forces and/or leader. This wording is brief and does not independently settle every duration or exception. November FAQ p7–8 clarifies ordinary Karama effects, which must not be confused with this special power. WBC 2022's explicitly amended tournament reference describes full-turn normal-revival prevention and a Ghola-card exception, but is not itself proof of target GF9 rules. The current full-turn scope, guaranteed declaration window, Ghola/Emperor-special exceptions, interactions with prior partial revival and later CHOAM/card-prevention effects remain explicitly provisional audit items. Expansion starts remain disabled; do not label the full Tleilaxu rules certified.
- Eleven focused special-power tests cover private/public windows independent of holdings, ownership and card validation, immutable rejected actions, permanent one-use tracking, stopped force/leader/KH/elite transactions, private negotiated requests, continuation into ordinary response chains, Emperor-funded payment/quota accounting, card exceptions, public-knowledge AI policies and complete blocked-phase progression. Existing revival and foreign-ghola test helpers now explicitly allow the new decision where those tests exercise a completed revival. Final verification: all 395 engine/AI/reference tests and all four persisted multiplayer journeys pass. TypeScript, lint and the production build pass after a test-only const correction. No browser visual QA or deployment; the original goal remains active.

- Next concrete expansion work: Ixian cyborg/suboid force model and casualty substitution, then setup/auction technology and Hidden Mobile Stronghold. Printed Ix p8–9 establishes 7 cyborgs/13 suboids, initial 3+3 in the mobile stronghold, cyborg strength 2/movement 2/collection 3/revival 3, suboid strength one-half and post-loss substitution. The current combat model assumes normal strength one (or unsupported one-half); it must be generalized before Ix is enabled.

## Ixian combat and suboid substitution checkpoint

- Generalized `CombatForces` with a fixed-half-strength normal-force mode. Ixian suboids always contribute one-half in basic and advanced play and cannot consume spice support. Basic cyborgs contribute two; advanced cyborgs contribute one unsupported or two for one spice. Other factions preserve their existing basic/advanced arithmetic. `validatePlan`, casualty resolution and AI now accept the Ixian half-strength basic dial range, with matching human/prescience input controls.
- Ixian leader/force identity stays within the existing physical-force model: seven cyborgs are tracked as a subset of twenty total forces, with thirteen suboids as the remainder. The lobby initializes seven cyborgs; changing factions clears or restores that subset appropriately. Base-game start also clears stale force-type metadata. Mobile stronghold setup remains gated and unimplemented.
- Basic Ixian double-strength cancellation now enters the pre-plan Karama queue. The November FAQ specifically limits this cancellation to basic cyborg strength; advanced cyborg and suboid strength do not receive that response. Suboid substitution has its own separate cancellation window when the owner chooses the exchange.
- Winning Ixians choose matching physical casualties in both game modes. The engine records cyborg losses by sector. If surviving suboids remain, `ixSubstitution` allows a partial/full exchange or decline. Selected surviving suboids go to the tanks and an equal number of cyborgs lost in that battle return to their original casualty sectors. Total physical casualties and the seven-cyborg inventory are preserved; unrelated prior cyborg deaths cannot be recovered. The loss and recovery transaction serializes, rejects invalid sources/counts immutably and resumes winner card choices/tech transfers/capture/Face Dancers after its response.
- Added `components/ix-substitution.tsx` with sector/count controls, distinct suboid/cyborg casualty labels, dial guidance/tooltips, internal faction rules and a searchable `ix-forces` topic with the printed example. All four AI policies generate legal basic/advanced plans using the shared solver and can make legal substitutions. Relative strength and opponent force-type estimation still require calibration.
- Primary evidence: publisher Ix rulebook p9 (force strengths, fixed suboid support, movement/collection/revival, substitution) and p10 (two cyborgs plus six suboids dialing six, then sacrificing the two surviving suboids to retain both lost cyborgs); November 2020 official FAQ p7 (basic-only cyborg-strength cancellation, substitution cancellation, no suboid-strength effect). The genuine local PDF/text remains `/tmp/dune-rules/ix-official-mirror.pdf` and `.txt`. Exact multi-sector loss selection/substitution restoration, later mobile-stronghold sectors, additional card timing and expansion interactions remain audit items.
- Fifteen focused tests cover the printed example in both modes, independent exhaustive token assignments, unsupported/supported cyborgs, unboostable suboids, half-strength basic plans, physical/typed conservation, optional/partial/canceled substitution, ownership and input validation, multi-sector restoration, traitor destruction, all AI difficulties and lobby force-type cleanup. Final verification: all 410 engine/AI/reference tests and all four persisted multiplayer journeys pass. TypeScript, lint and the production build pass, including the final basic-Ixian 20.5-strength input-range correction. No browser visual QA or deployment; the original goal remains active and expansion starts remain gated.
- Next required Ixian work: cyborg movement and collection capacity, per-type revival pricing and free-revival selection/limits, complete Hidden Mobile Stronghold setup/occupancy/movement/protection/income, auction/setup technology and its ally/special Karama interactions. Combat support does not establish complete Ixian play.

## Ixian movement, collection and revival checkpoint

- Cyborgs in the selected group now allow two-territory movement, carrying accompanying suboids. Cyborgs left behind do not grant speed. Ornithopters still grant three to either type. A move needing cyborg speed stores the validated group/sector/type choices in `pendingIxMove`, opens a Karama response before removal, and resumes through shared `completeMove` if allowed. Cancellation preserves the army and movement opportunity but restricts cyborg speed for that movement index; a shorter move remains legal. Exact cancellation duration across Hajr and broader simultaneous movement windows remain audit items.
- Normal spice collection now counts cyborg capacity as three per token while suboids retain the normal two/three ornithopter rate. Collection remains sector-specific and excludes storm/advisors. This is ordinary ground collection; mobile-stronghold travel income is still unimplemented.
- `forceRevivalPrice` and `paidForceRevivalCost` share typed prices between engine, UI and AI. Each paid cyborg costs three spice and suboid two. The free allowance may be assigned to either type via an explicit free-cyborg count; the UI offers cyborg-first or suboid-first selection. Default allocation uses free cyborgs first. Mixed Tleilaxu discounts halve the paid total and round up; discount cancellation restores the original typed total before payment. This aggregate mixed-price rounding remains part of the complete cross-faction economy audit.
- `eliteRevivalRemaining` distinguishes the one-per-turn Sardaukar/Fedaykin restriction from cyborg inventory. Cyborgs may fill their ordinary force-revival allowance with multiple cyborgs, and Ghola can return several more without consuming the ordinary allowance. All paths retain physical and typed counts. The official November FAQ p2 names Sardaukar/Fedaykin explicitly; it does not impose their limit on cyborgs.
- Emperor-funded extra returns now validate and pay each selected force's actual type price, with no free allowance. The Emperor is still the payer and the ally's extra quota remains separate. Cyborgs therefore cost three each in the current implementation; the printed Emperor ability pays revival costs without a cyborg-specific exception. That expansion interaction remains an explicit primary-source audit item alongside later homeworld/CHOAM effects.
- Added seven-cyborg-capacity force selectors, movement/cost guidance, free-type selection, correct mixed price previews and typed selectors for Ghola cards and Emperor allied extras. The Emperor's own elite choice and its ally's extra-revival choice are separate UI state. AI revival candidates now enumerate affordable amounts/type mixes using the same prices and proper special-force allowance; existing movement candidates can use the newly legal speed and complete response windows. Stronger economic/movement cancellation strategy still needs calibration.
- Thirteen focused scenarios cover mixed/selected-only/multi-sector movement, saved continuation, canceled retry and shorter move, ornithopters, collection capacity, explicit free-type pricing, multiple cyborg returns/Ghola and base elite limits, Tleilaxu discounts/cancellation, Emperor funding/funds rejection, shipment count/type conservation and all AI difficulties. All 423 engine/AI/reference tests and all four persisted multiplayer journeys pass. Final TypeScript, lint and production build pass, including the explicit Ghola/Emperor ally selectors and a test-only const correction. No browser visual QA or deployment. The goal remains active and incomplete.

Remaining Ixian work: complete Hidden Mobile Stronghold setup/occupancy/entry/movement/protection/income, setup and auction technology, allied card replacement, special Karama and full interaction audit. Live expansion starts remain gated.

## Hidden Mobile Stronghold checkpoint

- Added room-local mobile-stronghold state and an explicit virtual territory at `hidden_mobile_stronghold:0`. `gameTerritories` includes it only after placement; `gameDistance` connects it through its pointing sector with a one-territory entry/exit cost. Shared printed board metadata and other rooms remain unchanged. Normal movement, battle enumeration, stronghold victory and allied co-occupation checks now enumerate room territories. Expansion starts remain gated.
- The future Ix setup branch assigns three cyborgs and three suboids inside, with four cyborgs and ten suboids in reserves. The initial placement decision occurs after first-storm destruction and before the Spice Blow opening/advanced Fremen forecast. It accepts canonical sectors in non-stronghold territories, including Polar Sink. Advanced BG setup cannot use an unplaced mobile stronghold.
- Later Storm initialization offers an occupied Ixian stronghold relocation before dials or the advanced storm-card reveal. Declining resumes that initialization once. A route consists of adjacent printed sector locations, charges one per territory transition, forbids duplicate locations and ends in a non-stronghold territory within three steps. The normalized declaration survives JSON persistence. A public Karama response precedes movement/income; cancellation preserves pieces, location and spice, consumes that opportunity and resumes storm initialization.
- Relocation keeps every occupant inside. Current collection interpretation takes up to two spice per **Ixian** force from each visited spice sector, including departure and arrival, optionally declined for the whole route. It does not grant cyborg/ornithopter collection bonuses inside. This per-pile capacity and the no-repeat/within-territory sector-route interpretation are explicitly **provisional pending further primary clarification**, not certified full compliance. Storm-sector traversal is corrected in the later checkpoint below using the explicit printed p11 answer: relocation into, out of or through storm is forbidden. Passage through printed strongholds and the relationship between entry cost and separate movement opportunities remain audit items.
- Ixians may ship directly inside at ordinary stronghold rates, regardless of controller, subject to normal capacity/alliance restrictions. Other factions, including Guild cross-shippers, cannot. Advanced BG accompaniment is supported. Ordinary forces enter/exit only through the pointing sector; a storm there blocks transit. Inside forces remain separate from outside territory worms/storms and battles. Captured control counts toward the controller's win but never grants relocation authority. Contested/advisor-only occupation does not grant a winning hold.
- Advanced Ix special Karama relocates at most two territories on its own Shipment and Movement turn, requires occupying Ixian forces, spends a real card once, and leaves normal shipment/movement counters intact. It uses the same provisional route/collection interpretation. Later special-power cancellation and expansion interactions remain audit items.
- Added an original SVG stronghold marker, dotted attachment line, destination/force overlays, a keyboard-native sector-route editor with undo/collection preview, public pending-route display, special-Karama route controls, tooltip and searchable partial rules topic. No player-facing external navigation was added. No browser visual QA was performed or authorized.
- All four AI levels can place and relocate via private projections, use profitable special relocation, and move armies into/out of the virtual territory. Fixed movement-candidate filtering so unreachable high-scoring destinations cannot crowd out all reachable options before the 24-candidate limit. Relative strength and strategic placement remain uncalibrated.
- Twenty new scenarios cover room isolation, serialized timing/cancellation, invalid placement/routes and rollback, direct/Guild/BG shipping, movement cost/storm obstruction, capacity, inside/outside worms/battles, controller/contested/advisor victory, captured/empty movement restrictions, advanced reveal ordering, special Karama, all AI levels and BG setup validation. **443 unit/AI/reference tests and 4 persisted multiplayer tests pass. TypeScript, lint and production build pass.** No deployment. Goal remains active and incomplete.
- Primary evidence: publisher Ix rulebook p8–10 (initial forces, mobile stronghold, BG accompaniment), p6 (special Karama) and November 2020 FAQ p7 (movement/collection cancellation), using the genuine publisher PDF mirror already recorded above. The currently indexed publisher copy at https://www.gf9.com/Portals/0/Documents/GF9/IxianAndTleilaxuRulebook.pdf confirms p9 wording. Do not adopt tournament/house amendments to fill the provisional items silently.
- Next concrete work: Ixian setup/auction technology and allied purchased-card replacement, including their Karama/timing/private-information handling; then complete Ix deck assembly and the remaining advanced/expansion audits. CHOAM/Richese and Ecaz/Moritani engines and optional modules, full rules compliance, art/presentation polish and final validated deployment are still required.

## Ixian setup and auction technology checkpoint

- Wired the future gated Ixian start path to draw one starting card per faction into a private choice. Selecting one shuffles and privately deals the remainder; Harkonnen then draws its extra card from the deck. Pending choice prevents setup completion, is owned by Ixians, and survives JSON serialization. The decision/dealing path is exercised in setup fixtures; public expansion starts still reject unfinished rules and are not claimed end-to-end complete.
- Beginning an Ixian bidding round offers a public Karama window before any cards are drawn or inspected. Allowing draws the eligible-bidder count plus one, then offers a private card/top-or-bottom choice. The remaining cards are shuffled for bidding. Canceling draws only the ordinary count, with no Ixian inspection. A full Ixian hand does not disable this faction advantage; zero eligible bidders skip it. The chosen deck end is publicly logged, while card identity remains private.
- The private projection records an unordered inspected pool. It never exposes the actual next card to Ixians or the pool to other factions. The record includes cards already auctioned and deduplicates an earlier purchase returned through technology; it is not an inferred view of remaining hidden hands. UI labels make this distinction explicit.
- Advanced technology is offered before each card's bidding and before Atreides' independent inspection response. Ixians commit a hand card without seeing the incoming card; a separate Karama window precedes the swap. Declining preserves the option for another card. Declaring uses the round's attempt even if canceled. Successful exchange preserves hand count and makes the replacement the card Atreides can inspect. Canceling pool inspection does not also cancel advanced substitution.
- After an ally purchases a card, the buyer may keep it or request replacement before Emperor income and Harkonnen's separate bonus draw. The replacement response belongs to Ixians and names the buyer publicly; only the buyer sees the purchased identity. Success discards that exact purchase and draws the top deck card privately. Spice payment remains settled exactly once, canceled/declined replacements preserve the purchase, and Karama-paid purchases qualify without Emperor income. Bonus cards do not recursively receive this purchased-card privilege.
- Existing Harkonnen special exchanges can interrupt these windows. A pending offered card that no longer belongs to Ixians skips the stale swap and resumes Atreides inspection; a stale purchased-card decision exposes no replacement and can be declined to settle the sale. These recovery paths preserve card ownership and progress. The exact legality/order of such interruptions remains a primary timing audit rather than certification of every interaction. Bene Gesserit Worthless conversion cancellation restores the suspended private Ixian response correctly.
- Added the `IxTechnology` decision component, setup-aware decision routing, inspected-pool details, tooltip, searchable partial technology rules, updated faction guidance/coverage page, and all four AI policies. AI chooses starting cards, prepares pools, evaluates offered cards against known pool values and replaces weak allied purchases; complete advanced bidding phases terminate through legal private projections at every level. Strategic strength remains uncalibrated.
- Twenty-one new technology tests cover pre-draw cancellation/privacy, eligible/full-hand counts, top/bottom placement, invalid/unauthorized choices, order-independent pool projection, pre-Atreides swaps, one-attempt timing, setup/Harkonnen conservation, paid/free/allied/Harkonnen purchase sequencing, Worthless reactions, stale-card recovery, empty/short decks and every AI level. Short-deck fallback permits a lone available card to auction without removing it; multi-card insufficient-pool behavior remains an explicit rules audit, alongside Richese/other auction modes and exact Amal phase-opening order.
- Primary evidence: publisher Ixians & Tleilaxu p8 (initial and bidding preparation), p9 (advanced substitution and alliance), p11 (explicit Harkonnen extra setup draw), and November 2020 FAQ p7 (three distinct cancelable advantages). Publisher URLs and the genuine mirror remain in the source ledger. No tournament amendments were adopted.

## Mobile stronghold storm correction

- The same printed Ix rulebook p11 explicitly answers **No** to relocation into, out of or through a storm. This contradicts the earlier provisional allowance. `validateMobileMove` now rejects storm departure, arrival and transit before any special card or resources are spent. AI route generation and the sector-route control share the restriction; an occupied stronghold whose pointer is currently in storm can remain in place until the storm clears.
- Ordinary stronghold storm obstruction also applies to battles inside: the battle check uses the pointing sector, not the virtual internal sector zero. Storm/worm casualty immunity remains. A contested stronghold covered by storm grants no winning control; uncontested control still counts like a printed stronghold. This follows the general stronghold obstruction clarification in the November 2020 FAQ p1; no separate HMS battle exception was found.
- Two added scenarios reject ordinary/special storm routes without mutation and ensure AI routes avoid storm, including blocked origins. The existing internal-battle test now verifies storm obstruction and resumed battle after the pointer clears. Updated tooltips, searchable rules and the earlier provisional ledger note.
- **466 unit/AI/reference tests and all 4 persisted multiplayer journeys pass. TypeScript, lint and production build pass.** The focused 21-test technology suite also passes after the final public deck-end log refinement. No browser visual QA or deployment. Goal remains active and incomplete.
- Next concrete work: audit and assemble the complete fourteen-card Ix expansion inventory, resolve remaining card/auction/mobile-stronghold interpretation gaps and integrate the advanced/base systems. CHOAM/Richese, Ecaz/Moritani, optional modules, full rules compliance, presentation/art refinement and final validated deployment remain required.

## Complete Ix treachery inventory checkpoint

- Added the six remaining ordinary-type cards: Hunter Seeker, Basilia Weapon, Shield, Snooper, Harvester and Kull Wahad. `ixDeck()` composes these with the six special battle cards, Thumper and Amal; `treacheryDeck(['ix'])` returns 47 distinct cards. Existing base and Ix IDs remain stable; factories return fresh instances. Repeating the Ix option does not duplicate the set. Requests for unimplemented CHOAM/Ecaz/unknown decks fail explicitly. The future gated start path now uses this deck assembler; public expansion starts remain disabled.
- Corrected the base poison at `treachery-7` from Basilia Weapon to Ellaca Drug. Projections and action processing normalize that exact legacy ID/name in hands, deck, discard, auction and private Ix pools after cloning state. Expansion Basilia retains its own name and identity; original input states and historical logs are untouched.
- AI prescience recognition now looks up the complete implemented card catalog, including the additional ordinary weapons and defenses. Ordinary rule kinds preserve matching defense, Voice, lasgun/Shield and advanced BG Worthless conversion behavior without special duplicate-name dispatch.
- The second Harvester exposed the old one-use-per-blow assumption. Current behavior allows each physical copy to double the open fresh blow again, leaves pre-existing spice unchanged, resets table acceptance, and tracks the number of plays. Storm still prevents placement. Worm destruction explicitly closes the Harvester window, so adding another copy cannot resurrect spice. A shared availability helper drives engine, AI and guidance. Ambiguous legacy `harvested` windows stay closed; old live base rooms have only one Harvester.
- **Multiple-Harvester timing and multiplier interpretation remain provisional pending primary card-face verification.** The card names/count are corroborated secondary inventory facts, with the publisher independently establishing the fourteen-card total. This step improves the implementation without claiming that every card interaction is certified. Full provenance and unresolved limits are in `docs/CARD_INVENTORY.md`.
- Added a searchable internal Ix deck topic, corrected stale faction/coverage descriptions and updated fresh-blow guidance. No external navigation or copied publisher art was added to the app. Harvester coverage is now honestly marked partial for the multi-copy audit.
- Nine new tests verify exact card names/counts/identities, independent factories, rule types/Voice/Karama, duplicate Harvester conservation and timing, worm/storm restrictions, authoritative Shield explosions and poison leader kills, immutable legacy-name normalization, all AI Harvester choices, and complete six-base-faction Hard/Brutal games with all 47 cards conserved. These full-deck games are engine simulations; the four persisted multiplayer journeys still cover the currently enabled base/tech-token modes.
- **475 unit/AI/reference tests and all 4 persisted multiplayer journeys pass. TypeScript, lint and production build pass.** No browser visual QA or deployment. Goal remains active and incomplete.

Designer captions/transcript and the located retailer card photo could not be retrieved, so no card-by-card primary inspection is claimed from them. The source audit explicitly avoids importing tournament changes or the compilation's incorrect Ellaca defense description.

Card-face audits, mobile-stronghold collection/entry interpretations, CHOAM/Richese, Ecaz/Moritani, skills/strongholds/homeworlds/Nexus/discovery, complete internal guidance, generated card/leader art and presentation polish, and final validated deployment all remain required.

## Truthtrance question-flow checkpoint

- Added `game/truthtrance.ts` with a persistent overlay (`priority → ask → answer`, plus `unknown` for retry/save). The original response, decision, auction, phase opening, storm/spice state and sealed battle remain in place, so completing a question does not reconstruct or replay their effects. The engine rejects unrelated actions while the overlay is active. Setup completion also waits for the overlay to finish.
- Competing declarations are collected before any question is asked, then sorted by storm order. A holder can declare either or both physical copies. Each definite answer discards exactly its own card; an unknown answer leaves that card available for a different question or later play. The original input state remains immutable, and JSON round-trips preserve pending questions.
- Structured facts cover exact named cards currently held and selected traitor identities, including Cheap Hero. AND/OR combinations expose only the combined Yes/No answer. The server rejects dishonest fact answers, forged targets/card identities, invalid answers and malformed or excessive predicate trees. Only the target's personalized projection includes the expected fact answer before publication; no hand, traitor list or matching disjunct is revealed to other seats.
- Freeform game-related questions accept public Yes/No/unknown responses. An explicit current-turn scope records promises for the table; **the engine does not yet enforce the meaning of freeform questions or arbitrary promises.** The app/reference state this limitation. Future-turn scope is rejected by the structured payload; natural-language meaning still relies on human players. Facts and promises are recorded with turn/phase and retained in authoritative history.
- The dedicated React action panel remains available above suspended phase/faction controls. Hand cards can declare Truthtrance during those windows. It offers named-card/traitor clauses, AND/OR, a freeform question field, public answer controls, retry/save and current-turn history. Existing internal rule navigation is used; no external links or copied publisher assets were added.
- All four AI levels pass priority, answer server-verified facts through their private view, ask a structured question when resolving their own declaration and save an unanswerable question. **AI freeform interpretation and proactive strategic use of Truthtrance remain unfinished.** Bots return unknown instead of inventing a freeform answer.
- Twelve focused tests cover storm-order races, separate two-copy disposal, privacy, dishonest/malformed actions, immutable interruption of five window types, sealed plans, JSON reconnects, unknown/retry/save, current-turn records, incomplete setup and all four AI levels. The existing persisted multiplayer journeys cover the unchanged authoritative action/version/private-seat boundary; a dedicated persisted Truthtrance journey is still required.
- Source: GF9 November 2020 FAQ p8, https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Dune-FAQ-Nov-2020.pdf (indexed primary text). It establishes any-time play, game-related yes/no questions, current-turn commitments and impossibility, AND/OR, storm order, and unknown/retry/save. The base rulebook p23 supports Truthtrance during Voice/prescience interactions. Exact printed card-face/immediate-pause wording and a general simultaneous-action timing audit still need primary verification; secondary tournament amendments were not adopted as certification.
- Remaining Truthtrance work: binding current-battle plans and current-turn shipment/other decisions with feasibility escape; arbitrary human commitments and disputes; AI interpretation, question selection and use of learned facts; guaranteed interrupt opportunities between currently atomic game effects; priority/timing audit and complete persisted multiplayer scenarios. Full base/advanced/expansion compliance remains incomplete, with advanced and expansion starts still gated.
- **Verified checkpoint: 487 unit/AI/reference tests + 4 persisted multiplayer journeys pass; TypeScript, lint and the final production build pass.** New form styling includes consistent dark controls, keyboard focus, grouped fact fields and wrapping for long questions. No browser visual QA or deployment was performed. The active full-game goal is not complete or blocked.

## Binding battle Truthtrance checkpoint

- Added `game/battle-promises.ts`: validated conditions for dial equality/ranges, spice support, named weapon/defense slots (including empty slots and duplicate physical cards), exact leader/Cheap Hero identity, Kwisatz use, and bounded AND/OR combinations. Definite current-battle answers create public constraints attached to that battle; the recorded question receives the actual territory from the server. Arbitrary freeform/current-turn promises remain available and explicitly unenforced.
- The engine searches full legal completions for an answer, including previous active Truthtrance conditions, Voice, prescience, card-role pairings, controlled leaders, support, elite strength and Ixian half-strength suboids. It only offers answers with a currently legal completion; a sealed plan fixes its answer without exposing its other elements or acquiring new mutable commitments. The search consults the questioned player's private choices and public opponent properties, never the opponent's hidden plan or hand.
- Battle-plan validation enforces all active answers while leaving unrelated elements free. AND/OR conditions preserve alternative completions instead of secretly selecting and locking a witness. Contradictory later answers and prescience values are rejected. The prescience existence check now uses full-plan search when Truthtrance restricts an unshown element, removing the old assumption that all other numeric values can be zero.
- Integration exposed and fixed an existing basic Ixian prescience bug: the API now accepts legal half-point dials for Ixians, matching the existing UI and battle validator. A test specifically reveals 2.5 under a bound Ixian dial.
- A pending Voice response resolves before impossibility releases are considered, so a canceled Voice retains the original answer. After an opposing effect completes, promises are evaluated in their declaration order; an impossible one is publicly released while feasible earlier obligations remain. Canceling Fremen free support and elite strength participates in this search. Voluntary card spending, bribes and aid pledges that would make the actor's still-feasible battle promise impossible are rejected atomically.
- Personalized views expose answer options only to the target, and a legal example plan only to its owner. The React interface adds battle condition fields, public bindings/release status, and a private preview that can populate the editable battle form without sealing it. Tooltips and the internal rules explain the feature and its limits. All four bot levels can choose a feasible answer, filter their candidate plans against existing commitments, and use an authoritative private completion when their heuristic candidate set lacks a required exact dial/support/Kwisatz choice.
- Seventeen new tests cover immutable/public-versus-private state, overlapping Yes/No answers, OR alternatives, dual-role Ix pairs, sealed plans, prescience, pending/canceled/completed Voice, voluntary spice/card evasion, optional Kwisatz flags, duplicate cards and Cheap Heroes, basic Ix half dials, malformed payloads, all four AI levels, Fremen/elite cancellations, and comparison of offered answers against an exhaustive authoritative basic-plan oracle. The full Truthtrance-focused group is 29 tests.
- **Still partial:** feasibility explores currently available plans, not multi-action futures such as reviving a currently dead leader before committing, additional Karama/card sequences, or acquiring a new choice later. Released answers currently stay released; exact revalidation behavior, cancellation timing, self-caused impossibility and multi-promise ordering need a primary-source interaction audit. Guaranteed interrupt opportunities between atomic engine actions also remain unfinished. Do not call this full Truthtrance compliance.
- Source basis remains the official GF9 November 2020 FAQ p8 (current-turn obligations, impossibility, AND/OR, storm order) and base rulebook p23 (Truthtrance during Voice/prescience and freedom to alter unaffected plan components). Tournament amendments requiring reactivation of previously invalid answers are not silently treated as the printed publisher rule.
- Next work: current-turn shipment and broader binding decisions, future-action feasibility/card-sequence audit and persisted Truthtrance game scenarios; complete remaining base/advanced interactions and all expansion faction/module engines. CHOAM/Richese, Ecaz/Moritani, optional skills/strongholds/homeworlds/Nexus/discovery, AI calibration, complete guidance, generated art/AAA polish and final validated deployment remain part of the active goal. Advanced/expansion starts remain gated.
- **Verified checkpoint: 504 unit/AI/reference tests and all 4 persisted multiplayer journeys pass; TypeScript, lint and the final production build pass.** No browser visual QA or deployment. The full-game goal remains active and incomplete.

## Reachable Ghola battle preparation checkpoint

- Expanded battle-answer feasibility from currently legal plans to plans reachable through actual Ghola cards in the questioned player's hand. A bounded-by-card-consumption search explores eligible controlled dead leaders, Atreides' dead Kwisatz, and valid force/elite revival choices. Each physical card is consumed in its simulated branch, so one Ghola cannot revive both a leader and KH. Revived forces remain in reserves and never inflate the current battle's army.
- Extracted `applyGholaEffect` from the existing card dispatcher so real play and lookahead share revival/control/quota/income logic. The search uses independent state clones and never spends a real card, changes a real leader or leaks rival hands/plans. Selected preparations are suggestions, not locks: other legal ways to satisfy an OR condition remain available. Diverting the last needed Ghola to an incompatible revival is rejected atomically by existing promise enforcement.
- A pending Tleilaxu revival-income response is modeled as a possible future receipt, not currently spendable spice. This includes the original income response suspended beneath a BG Worthless Karama conversion. Promise feasibility waits for the outcome; allowing income enables the supported plan, while successful cancellation releases an otherwise impossible answer without undoing the actual revival. Another faction's income and deferred tech-token income cannot fund the questioned player's current battle.
- Prescience also recognizes Ghola-reachable leaders, including when there is no preceding Truthtrance. A player's personalized battle view exposes `compliantPreparation` (needed card actions, possible plan, income-wait flag) separately from `compliantPlan`, which remains immediately legal. The React guide offers a real Ghola play, disables it during other pending windows, and leaves final plan review/sealing separate. All four AI levels execute a required preparation before committing or answering a prescience element.
- Twelve tests cover untouched authoritative state and private projections, pending JSON reconnects, Ghola-to-leader completion, prescience with/without a prior promise, physical-card limits, KH/hero dependencies, native/captured/foreign-ghola control, incompatible diversions and OR alternatives, Tleilaxu income and real/Worthless Karama outcomes, unavailable third-party funding, reserve-force conservation, all four AI levels and sealed-plan immutability. The full Truthtrance-related focused group now contains 41 tests.
- Primary evidence: official base rulebook FAQ p22, https://www.gf9games.com/dunegame/wp-content/uploads/Dune-Rulebook.pdf, explicitly permits a leader revived by Ghola during Battle to fight in that Battle Phase. The November 2020 FAQ p8 supplies the Truthtrance current-turn obligation/impossibility principle. KH and foreign-ghola treatment reuse the existing engine's revival rules; their outstanding broader source audit is not claimed complete by this step. Older PBEM/tournament restrictions forbidding same-phase Ghola use were not adopted.
- Still required: other future card/power sequences, precise interruption and reactivation rules, current-turn shipment/other obligations and freeform commitment enforcement, dedicated persisted Truthtrance journeys, remaining base/advanced rules and every expansion/module. This addition does not enable advanced/expansion starts or establish full rules compliance. No browser visual QA or deployment was performed.
- **Verified checkpoint: 516 unit/AI/reference tests and all 4 persisted multiplayer journeys pass; the 41-test Truthtrance group also passes after expanding AI prescience scenarios. TypeScript, lint and the final production build pass.** The full-game goal remains active and incomplete.

## CHOAM charity and hand-limit checkpoint

- Publisher CHOAM & Richese rulebook pp. 7–8 and p. 12 cancellation table, verified through the indexed official PDF: https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/CHOAM-Rulebook-low-res.pdf . CHOAM takes two spice for each faction, including itself, before other claims. Other factions take their charity from CHOAM. Karama cancels the income and sends charity to the bank, with ordinary charity still available to eligible CHOAM. Printed hand limit is five.
- Added `game/charity.ts` for shared entitlement and payer selection. `beginPhase` opens a public CHOAM-income response after any Ix/Amal phase opening. Settlement persists the turn and cancellation state. Ordinary and advanced Bene Gesserit claims transfer actual spice from CHOAM; wealthy Bene Gesserit still has its own cancellation window before any transfer. Cancellation restores bank payments for this turn only. Claimed charity continues to trigger existing Spice Production rules.
- Private projection exposes only the current player's entitlement and the public payer/status. Other players' spice and claim flags remain private. Shared hand limit now supports CHOAM's fifth purchase, and the hand tab displays current count/capacity. UI names the income response and payer; internal rules include a linked, explicitly partial CHOAM economy topic. All four bot levels resolve the response and claim eligible charity; Brutal may spend Karama on enemy income and preserves allied income.
- Fifteen focused scenarios in `tests/choam-charity.test.ts` cover income timing, ordinary/advanced payout conservation, both cancellation layers, next-turn reset, Amal order and JSON roundtrip, six-player funding, private views, deferred tech income, fifth-card purchase/full-hand Karama exclusion, AI and retained start gates. Existing Bene Gesserit and reference suites pass alongside them (29 focused tests).
- Deliberate unresolved boundary: the publisher text does not explain insolvency if CHOAM spends away its funds before a claim. The engine rejects an unfunded payout atomically rather than inventing a bank subsidy or negative spice. This is not certified full compliance. Inflation, whether CHOAM's automatic income itself triggers Spice Production, self-claims after spending income, and all later module interactions need source audits. CHOAM remains gated.
- Additional primary findings for upcoming work: start two spice/twenty reserves/free revival zero; paid force revival one spice and unlimited. Karama limits it to three (five with Tleilaxu permission) at two spice each. Mentat Inflation places a Double/Cancel token for the following turn, flips at the next Mentat, then is removed from the game at the subsequent Mentat. Karama prevents initial placement, never the mandatory flip. Double face in play prohibits bribes. Exact doubled ordinary charity entitlement, combined Tleilaxu revival discounts and insolvency remain to verify.
- Remaining CHOAM powers: end-phase duplicate sales, Worthless sales/six special effects, once-per-turn two-way allied card trade, allied battle support, advanced Auditor and battle-support income, special Karama, printed leader data and full setup. Auditor checks two random unused opposing hand cards if alive, one if killed (not win/loss); opponent may pay one spice per card to cancel the audit. It cannot be acquired as a foreign ghola by Tleilaxu, be captured or gain a leader skill; CHOAM’s own Ghola card revival is not prohibited (corrected by the 2026-09-07 Auditor audit). The p. 12 cancellation table also covers card sales/Worthless effects, single-battle force income and the audit.

- Validation for this checkpoint: all 531 unit/AI/reference tests, all four persisted multiplayer journeys, TypeScript, lint and production build pass. Logs: `/tmp/dune-unit-check.log`, `/tmp/dune-multiplayer-check.log`, `/tmp/dune-type-check.log`, `/tmp/dune-lint-check.log`, `/tmp/dune-build-check.log`. No browser QA or deployment performed.

## CHOAM force revival checkpoint

- Primary rulebook p. 7: no native free revivals, no quantity limit for paid forces, one spice per force. Primary p. 12 Karama table: restore three-force limit (five if Tleilaxu permits) and two-spice price. Indexed official PDF verified again: https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/CHOAM-Rulebook-low-res.pdf . Genuine Ix rulebook mirror text `/tmp/dune-rules/ix-official-mirror.txt` p. 6 states allied force/leader revival at half price rounded up.
- `RevivalRules.choamBlocked` tracks this combined CHOAM cancellation separately from Tleilaxu quantity/discount cancellation and resets on entering Revival. Shared `forceRevivalPrice` accepts the cancellation status; CHOAM's native cost precedes any separate Tleilaxu discount. `forceRevivalLimit` preserves native CHOAM unlimited revival when Tleilaxu permissions are canceled.
- `choamRevival` response belongs to CHOAM and precedes spending/movement. If canceled, a request beyond the newly applicable total allowance aborts without partial payment or revival. A valid request is repriced; insufficient funds abort without changes. Prior revivals remain completed and count against the new total allowance. Applicable Tleilaxu five-force/discount response windows follow. Canceling only Tleilaxu's discount retains active CHOAM native price. This avoids treating the two independent abilities as a single generic half-price flag.
- Free Fremen allowance remains free and is tracked across requests; a completely free request within the normal three-force allowance does not invoke CHOAM's paid/quantity advantage. Emperor extras retain their existing two-spice price and separate allowance. Tleilaxu income receives actual payment, and advanced Tleilaxu special prevention occurs before the CHOAM response. No leader revival prices changed.
- Private views expose the cancellation flag for the player's force quote; UI displays native pricing and the separate allied discount. All four AI levels use the shared quote before spending. Internal CHOAM reference now links to its force-revival topic; remaining powers and faction start gates are retained.
- Seventeen scenarios in `tests/choam-revival.test.ts` verify quantities through twenty, cancellation/repricing/affordability, independent powers, actual Tleilaxu income, free and Emperor extras, next-phase reset, earlier completed revival, Tleilaxu prevention, JSON reconnect and private projections, AI budgeting and force/spice conservation. All 53 focused CHOAM/Tleilaxu/Ix economy/reference tests pass.
- Interpretations still needing final combined-expansion audit: applying Tleilaxu half-price to CHOAM's native one-spice rate, aggregate rounding per declared transaction, cancellation recovery by repricing versus a fresh player confirmation, and exceptions introduced by Recruits/homeworlds/leader skills. These tests verify the implementation, not publication certification of unresolved interactions. Full CHOAM starts remain disabled. Printed CHOAM leader data, Auditor, Inflation, Worthless powers/sales, ally trades/support and special Karama remain required.

## CHOAM Inflation checkpoint

- Verified primary CHOAM rulebook p. 8 Inflation and p. 12 cancellation table at https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/CHOAM-Rulebook-low-res.pdf . Place either side during Mentat; the following turn doubles/cancels all charity including CHOAM income and advanced Bene Gesserit; next Mentat flips; subsequent Mentat removes from the game. Double face in play prohibits bribes. Only initial placement can be canceled by Karama.
- Newly located primary FAQ p. 10 resolves an earlier open question: CHOAM income alone does NOT trigger Spice Production, and Inflation does NOT affect the tech-token payment. Existing separation is retained and directly tested. Same page also confirms Auditor payment is bounded by cards actually available and cannot be partial, and duplicate-card sales require identical cards (Shield Snooper/Portable Snooper do not match Snooper). Record these for subsequent CHOAM work.
- Added persistent `Inflation` with side, placement turn, update turn and flip flag; `inflationUsed` prevents reuse after removal; `inflationAttemptTurn` prevents retry in a canceled placement phase. `choamInflation` action is available to CHOAM during Mentat, clears table readiness and opens a public Karama response. Successful placement spends the token; cancellation preserves it for a later Mentat.
- Shared `charityMultiplier` modifies all ordinary and advanced BG claims and CHOAM per-faction income beginning the turn after placement. Cancel records charity initialization without creating a zero-income response; all claim amounts are zero, including wealthy advanced BG. Karama cancellation of CHOAM income retains Inflation and pays doubled ordinary charity from the bank. BG cancellation still resolves before any charge to CHOAM.
- `beginPhase` flips/removes the token during Mentat after the existing Ix/Amal opening, without a cancelable response. `updatedTurn` prevents duplicate advancement. Existing bribes still settle; all new bribe actions are rejected while Double is active, including before the first affected Charity phase. Optional placement occurs during normal Mentat controls after existing automatic victory settlement; a finished game needs no subsequent-turn token placement.
- UI shows the public side, affected turn, next lifecycle event and Double bribe prohibition, plus CHOAM placement buttons/save-by-ready guidance and response explanations. Private entitlement and projected income reflect the multiplier. AI levels choose an initial side using their own wealth/forces (Easy varies); Brutal may cancel enemy Double placement. Bots skip suppressed charity and finish their normal phase actions.
- Fourteen scenarios in `tests/choam-inflation.test.ts` cover both sequences, once-per-game removal, phase/owner/side validation, canceled placement/retry, ordinary/advanced payouts, both cancellation layers, no repeated flips, bribes and deferred payments, unchanged tech income, Amal ordering, persistence, private/public status and all four AI policies. All 43 focused Inflation/charity/BG/reference tests pass.
- Explicit interpretation still under audit: ordinary charity eligibility remains zero or one spice, and Double multiplies the received amount (four from zero, two from one), rather than changing the threshold or automatically raising wealth to four. Primary wording says charity is doubled but the one-spice example was not located. Canceled placement's later-turn retry and full interactions with homeworlds, leader skills, end-phase sales/trades and other unimplemented modules also require final combined-expansion audit. Complete CHOAM faction starts remain gated.

## CHOAM end-phase sales and allied exchange checkpoint

- Verified publisher rulebook p. 7 Treachery and p. 8 Alliance: exact duplicates may be revealed and surplus cards discarded for three spice each at phase end; Worthless cards may be discarded for two spice. One two-way allied card exchange per game turn, at the end of any phase. Primary p. 10 FAQ rejects Shield Snooper/Portable Snooper as duplicates of Snooper. Primary p. 12 cancellation table says prevent discarding a card for spice. Official indexed source: https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/CHOAM-Rulebook-low-res.pdf . No tournament changes adopted.
- `Game.choamMarket` provides an always-present closing decision when CHOAM is in a playing table, independent of hidden hand contents. Normal `nextPhase` now opens this window; `advancePhase` performs the original continuation only when CHOAM finishes. Storm's separate continuation is handled by `resume: storm` and `advanceAfterStorm`, preserving the existing forecast/opening sequence. Automatically skipped phases still receive their closing opportunity.
- `game/choam-market.ts` builds private sale choices and validates exact-name witnesses. Any distinct matching copy may be retained. CHOAM declares one physical sale at a time; its card (and duplicate name where applicable) is publicly revealed, then a `choamSale` Karama response precedes discard and payment. Cancellation leaves the card in hand and blocks that physical card from another sale in this closing window. Other cards may still be sold.
- `choamTradeReply` lets the ally choose a return card or decline; `choamTradeConfirm` gives CHOAM final approval or decline. Successful exchange atomically swaps the two cards, preserves hand sizes and spends `choamTradeTurn`. Declines do not consume the turn's successful trade. The market tracks an attempted offer only to avoid AI repeat loops; the human may renegotiate. Trade cards and sale choices appear only in authorized private projections; public decisions contain no card identities.
- Transactions revalidate card custody at settlement. An interrupted sale with a missing card/witness pays nothing; an impossible trade moves neither card. Truthtrance remains available as an overlay and is tested consuming a pending sale card. Other special powers are currently held until the closing decision completes; bots suppress those competing special-card choices while the market exists.
- `components/choam-market.tsx` provides sale, offer, reply, confirm/decline and phase-finish controls. UI names all new windows. AI sells eligible surplus/Worthless cards, offers a private exchange, handles empty hands/declines, and finishes the phase; higher levels compare the offered/returned values. Internal reference adds the linked CHOAM market topic. Existing CHOAM test helpers now explicitly finish closing decisions rather than assuming an immediate phase transition.
- Twenty-two focused scenarios verify ownership/timing, invariant public windows, delayed sale payment, exact duplicates and arbitrary retained witness, failed/forged sales, Karama/card preservation, private two-way consent, declines/empty hands, custody changes, tech income and auction escrow settlement, storm/automatic battle/auction continuations, persistence, Truthtrance interruption, AI progress and once-per-turn trade across boundaries. All 66 market/CHOAM/reference focused tests pass.
- Remaining audit boundaries: multi-card simultaneous sales versus serial declarations, whether Karama blocks only the selected sale card or the whole sale power for that phase, required ordering among concurrent end-phase income/refund/sale/trade effects, and other anytime card/special-power interactions in the closing window. The current implementation prioritizes the market before deferred tech income and escrow refunds. Full CHOAM starts remain gated. Worthless special effects, allied battle support, Auditor, printed leaders, special Karama and other expansion modules remain required.

- Validation: all 580 unit/AI/reference tests, four persisted multiplayer journeys, TypeScript, lint and production build pass. All check processes exited successfully. Logs remain in `/tmp/dune-unit-check.log`, `/tmp/dune-multiplayer-check.log`, `/tmp/dune-type-check.log`, `/tmp/dune-lint-check.log` and `/tmp/dune-build-check.log`. No browser QA or deployment performed.

## CHOAM special Karama cash-in checkpoint

- Publisher CHOAM & Richese rulebook p. 4 permits the special Karama to discard hand Treachery Cards, including Worthless cards, for three spice apiece at any time. Official indexed source: https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/CHOAM-Rulebook-low-res.pdf . Implementation applies the existing advanced-game, real-Karama and once-per-game special-power restrictions. The activation Karama is discarded separately and earns no income; a second Karama may be among the selected cash-in cards. The activation-card interpretation and exact interruption boundaries still require final source audit.
- New shared `game/choam-karama.ts` filters available cards against the player's sealed battle plan and exact prescience commitments. The authoritative action requires distinct owned physical IDs, at least one selected card and a separate activation card; invalid requests are atomic. It preserves a winning auction's funding using available spice plus the auction's actually committed allied payment, or another retained real Karama. Uncommitted ally pledges never count as already committed funding.
- CHOAM may cash in across all nine playing phases, phase-opening decisions, responses, pending revival and market/trade decisions, preserving the interrupted continuation. An active Truthtrance question still resolves first. A pending sale or trade whose offered card was cashed in is revalidated and aborts without duplicate income or partial exchange. Private projections expose only the acting player's eligible cards; public logs reveal counts and income rather than discarded identities.
- `components/choam-cash-in.tsx` provides activation selection, eligible-card checkboxes and a live gain preview above the private hand. It excludes stale/committed selections and prevents submission during an active Truthtrance question. Internal faction/reference topics explain the implemented power and its outstanding audit boundaries without external player-facing links.
- Battle-plan reachability now explores actual cash-in preparations alongside Ghola through shared authoritative effects on independent clones. Each branch consumes physical cards and the once-per-game power; retained promised weapons/defenses and leaders remain necessary for the resulting legal plan. The existing battle guide shows the required cash-in and lets the player execute the next preparation. All four AI levels can follow a binding promise that needs the proceeds, then seal a compliant plan. Outside battles, bots sell low-value Worthless cards when short of spice while preserving committed auction funding.
- Fourteen tests cover all nine phases, pricing and once-only use, ordinary disposal of an extra Karama, atomic malformed/forged selections, advanced-game restrictions, response/opening preservation, actual Amal order, funding an interrupted revival, invalidated sales/trades, winning-bid funding, sealed/prescience reservations, private JSON projections, all four AI policies and a binding battle promise requiring both cash-in-funded support and a retained defense. The focused cash-in/Ghola/market suite has 44 passing tests.
- Remaining work includes other future battle preparation sequences, special-power timing and activation-card source clarification, CHOAM Worthless effects, allied battle support/income, Auditor and printed leaders, plus remaining factions/modules and full interaction audits. ## CHOAM combat funding and force-payment income checkpoint

- Primary CHOAM & Richese rulebook p. 8 verified through the publisher's indexed PDF: https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/CHOAM-Rulebook-low-res.pdf . Its alliance rule permits paying some or all of an ally's forces in battle. Advanced Forces pays CHOAM half of other players' force payments, rounded down; its own payments go to the bank, and any revealed traitor prevents the income. Primary p. 12 permits Karama to cancel force-payment collection for one battle. The base November 2020 FAQ p. 7 permits cancellation of alliance powers. Direct publisher PDF fetches remain 403; no local genuine CHOAM PDF was obtained.
- Extended existing authoritative allied escrow to advanced CHOAM during Battle. A new `choamBattleFunding` decision gives CHOAM a guaranteed chance to set its pledge before ordinary battle preparation begins. Ordinary phase controls can adjust the unspent pledge. A mutual alliance is required; other factions cannot pledge combat funds. A `choamBattleAid` response precedes sealing and can block the alliance benefit for that battle without consuming the escrow. Later battles get fresh funding/response opportunities.
- `Plan.allyPayment` records the sealed CHOAM share separately from total support; it is not a prescience-selectable field. The default split spends the combatant's own spice first, then covers its shortfall from eligible escrow. Explicit shares may use more CHOAM funding. Validation bounds total support by actual available resources, rejects foreign/oversized shares, and prevents withdrawal of a committed contribution. Bribes reserve only the combatant's own committed share. Final payment uses the same atomic allied-payment helper as bidding/shipment.
- Unused combat escrow returns after the Battle closing market, alongside the existing phase-end settlement. Pledges remain private to donor/recipient views; an ally does not gain access to the other's spice or sealed plan. Private legal-plan search, prescience feasibility and binding Truthtrance completion now include actual eligible CHOAM funds. Pledged spice cannot bypass a canceled alliance benefit or become a general-purpose transfer.
- Battle resolution records pending CHOAM income from each non-CHOAM combatant's own payment, rounded down separately. CHOAM's own support and the amounts it pays for its ally are excluded. A sole traitor winner spends neither own nor allied support; mutual traitors still pay both commitments. Any traitor suppresses all CHOAM income. An explosion without a traitor still produces normal paid support and income. After casualty/winner-card choices, `choamBattleIncome` opens a response before technology/capture/Face Dancer continuations. Allowing pays once; canceling sends the amount to the bank without refunding combatants.
- Added private funding controls, payment-split inputs, response explanations, revealed/compliant-plan payment details, linked internal combat reference and tooltip. All four AI levels allocate CHOAM support before allied battles and use it in legal plan generation, including binding support promises. Hard/Brutal may counter an enemy alliance benefit; Brutal may cancel enemy force income. No player-facing external links were added.
- Twenty new scenarios cover funding ownership/timing, mutual alliances, affordability and malformed requests, locked payment shares, explicit splits, per-payer rounding, once-only income, real and BG Worthless Karama responses, canceled funding across two battles, phase refunds, one/two traitors, CHOAM's own payments, Fremen free support, basic-game exclusion, explosions, private projections and JSON reconnects, preserved Atreides full-plan offers, and all four AI levels including a five-spice binding promise with zero own spice. The focused combat/CHOAM Karama/full-plan group has 54 passing tests.
- Remaining audit boundaries: per-player versus combined rounding when both pay odd amounts, exclusion of CHOAM-funded allied contributions under the own-payment clause, exact timing of the alliance cancellation and income versus simultaneous post-battle powers, and later module interactions. The implementation follows the literal payment interpretation, but these edge cases lack explicit publisher examples. Worthless special effects, Auditor/printed leaders, other factions/modules and full-game interaction work remain. Advanced/expansion starts stay gated.

- Validation: all 614 unit/AI/reference tests and all four persisted multiplayer journeys pass; TypeScript, lint and final production build pass. The first full run found only an existing case-sensitive error-message assertion, corrected by restoring compatible wording; the entire suite then passed. All check processes exited successfully.
