## CHOAM Kulon and La La La checkpoint

- Verified publisher CHOAM & Richese rulebook p. 7 and cancellation table p. 12 through indexed official PDF: https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/CHOAM-Rulebook-low-res.pdf . Kulon adds one territory on CHOAM's Shipment and Movement turn. La La La prevents a player's Free Revival during Revival. Karama prevents discarding a Worthless card in that phase for its special effect. Both are basic faction advantages and also work in advanced play.
- Added a shared CHOAM Worthless declaration/settlement path with `pendingChoamWorthless` and the `choamWorthless` Karama response. The physical owned card and declared target are validated before announcing. Discard occurs only on successful settlement. Cancellation retains the card and tracks its physical ID as blocked for that phase. Card custody is rechecked: if special Karama cash-in consumes the pending card, no special effect occurs and an interrupted revival continues.
- Kulon requires CHOAM's active movement turn and an available move. Its turn-stamped bonus adds to normal range and ornithopters; it does not grant extra actions or bypass storm, occupancy or allied-entry validation. The current interpretation applies the bonus to both available Hajr movements. A later turn ignores the old bonus. Private views now include the actual number of allowed movement actions for controls/AI.
- Added `freeRevivalRemaining` shared by authoritative revival quotes and the private projection; UI and bots use that projected entitlement. `RevivalRules.freeBlocked` denies ordinary free forces for the rest of Revival, including a Fremen allied allowance granted before or after the denial. Normal paid revival and separate Ghola card returns remain possible. Earlier force returns and quota consumption stay unchanged; the next Revival initialization clears denial.
- Every CHOAM table opens a `choamFreeRevival` decision when another faction declares a normal force revival with a free portion, independently of CHOAM's private hand. CHOAM may allow the request or declare La La La. Success stops the entire pending request without payment, force movement or quota use; the requester can explicitly select a paid revival afterward. Cancellation/missing card resumes the original quote through the existing Tleilaxu special-prevention window and later revival responses. This ordering avoids automatic spending at a newly increased price.
- New `components/choam-worthless.tsx` supplies private phase/reaction controls, target selection and allow/pass. Phase UI shows extra movement range and free-revival denial, and a linked internal topic/tooltip explains the two implemented cards. All AI levels use Kulon when movement is available. Easy saves La La La; higher levels may deny an enemy's free request while preserving allied requests. AI revival budgets use the denied entitlement rather than faction defaults.
- Sixteen new scenarios cover basic/advanced range, ornithopters, storm/alliance restrictions, Hajr action counts and next-turn expiry, ownership/phase/timing rejection, Karama retention/retry, earlier free returns, hand-independent public decisions, mixed request aborts, cancellation resumption, cash-in interruption, later Fremen grants, Ghola exception, Tleilaxu continuation, next-phase reset, private JSON reconnects, AI policies/budgets and unsupported/forged effects. The focused Worthless/CHOAM revival group has 33 passing tests. One preexisting revival scenario now explicitly allows CHOAM's new free-revival decision before testing Tleilaxu cancellation.

Outstanding interpretations include physical-card versus whole-power Karama scope, Kulon with Hajr/ornithopters, reactive La La La request cancellation versus repricing, ordering with other prevention effects and later free-revival exceptions. ## Trip to Gamont and deferred CHOAM Mentat victory checkpoint

- Primary CHOAM & Richese p. 7 permits returning any one force belonging to another player to its reserves during Mentat Pause; p. 12 supplies the Worthless-effect Karama cancellation. Official indexed PDF: https://www.gf9games.com/dune/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/CHOAM-Rulebook-low-res.pdf . Primary base p. 11 checks occupied strongholds during Mentat; November 2020 FAQ p. 1 excludes unresolved opponent co-occupation and discusses ties with extra strongholds/tech. The ordering below ensures a legal Mentat return can affect the board used for victory; the detailed schedule is an implementation interpretation requiring later interaction audit.
- Extended the CHOAM Worthless declaration with Trip to Gamont, target player, board sector and ordinary/elite choice. Rejects self, absent player/force, reserves, invalid sector, unavailable force type and quantities other than one. The public response includes the selected recipient, sector and force type. Settlement rechecks both card and typed force, then moves exactly one token to that player's reserves. Elite totals/type are preserved; no tanks, battle-loss count, revival allowance, spice or transport income changes.
- The return can select forces in storm, Bene Gesserit advisors or occupants of the Hidden Mobile Stronghold. Returning a stronghold occupant does not relocate the stronghold. Existing advisor stance settlement runs after the return so removal of the last opposing force is reflected in control. A missing card/force aborts without an extra discard or reserve token. Karama keeps the selected force and card, blocking that physical card's effect for the phase.
- At tables containing CHOAM, `beginPhase(8)` still settles bribes and Inflation once but defers victory. Ordinary Mentat actions and readiness lead to the existing closing market. `advancePhase` then opens a hand-independent `choamMentat` decision; a newly exchanged Trip card remains usable before victory. After each Trip response, this final decision reopens. CHOAM's explicit completion calls victory on the resulting board and only advances the turn if play continues. Other tables retain the existing entry-time victory behavior. `completePhase` isolates that continuation without repeating settlement or the market.
- New `components/choam-gamont.tsx` offers owner/sector/type choices and final victory confirmation. Public Mentat guidance explains the delay; internal faction, phase and Worthless topics now link to a detailed Trip to Gamont topic. All four AI levels can select and return an enemy force and finish the window. Higher levels prioritize single stronghold occupants and elite forces. Bots preserve a usable Mentat Trip from proactive cash-in, market sale and their own trade offer so the final chance is not accidentally lost.
- Nineteen new tests cover deferred/unchanged winning boards, ordinary/advanced returns, public empty/full-hand windows, readiness, Karama, ordinary/elite conservation, invalid actions, storm/advisors, automatic advisor stance, mobile stronghold occupants, closing allied acquisition, interrupted card/force custody, JSON reconnects, final-turn Guild fallback/BG prediction, once-only bribes/Inflation, tech-token victory and all four AI policies. The focused Gamont/market/Inflation/reference suite passes 53 tests. The shared Worthless reconnect case now covers both La La La and Kulon; the first full run caught optional response fields serialized differently, fixed by emitting force/recipient fields only when applicable. One existing exchange-limit test now completes the additional Mentat decision before asserting the next turn.
- Remaining timing audit: exact ordering of sales/trades and Trip versus other simultaneous Mentat abilities, opportunities to revisit an already closed market after a canceled Trip, future force-type/stronghold/module exceptions, and the existing advisor-force cancellation rules. Baliset, Jubba Cloak and Kull Wahad remain unfinished, alongside Auditor/printed leaders, all other remaining factions/modules and complete-game audits. Advanced and expansion starts remain gated.

## Owner-proved seat recovery, visual combat and AI calibration

- Added authenticated recovery-key setup and private-proof recovery at `POST /api/rooms/:code/control`. Recovery preserves player identity, game state and sealed plans, increments the room version, revokes older seat credentials and installs the new token. Storage keeps cryptographic hashes; private keys and tokens do not enter `GameView` or public history. An exact operation receipt can reissue the current cookie without rotating twice; changed credentials/proof, later rotations and replaced keys invalidate older receipts.
- `SeatAuth` now contains player ID and token hash. Every authoritative game write checks that exact active credential in the same conditional SQL statement as the room version. Private reads join the live credential with the room. This fences requests authenticated before revocation as well as later reads. Additive Drizzle migrations `0001_fancy_king_cobra.sql` and `0002_famous_piledriver.sql` are applied locally; apply them before dependent code in other environments.
- Integrated `SeatRecoverySetup` below the table and `SeatRecoveryClaim` in the join area. The owner saves the complete generated private kit before enabling it; the setup retains that kit through uncertain and confirmed results. Recovery validates the saved kit and retains the exact operation ID/new session token for explicit retries. No automatic retry or localStorage secret storage. Normal game actions and management share the pending guard. Restored-seat integration isolates owner identity and discards stale epoch/version results, including WebMCP reads from an exited/recovered context.
- Initial invite failures now show a GET-only retry screen for transient errors and a usable join form for missing/auth-rejected seats. HTTP 408/5xx mutation errors are treated as uncertain. Independent client review confirmed both fixes and identified stale read return values; obsolete reads now reject instead of escaping through the WebMCP return value. Additional settlement guards verify seat/epoch as well as room.

## Initial room-entry recovery checkpoint

- Create and join now optionally accept a private operation receipt. The server hashes its operation, exact normalized request identity and session token, atomically stores the room/seat/receipt, and returns the current original private view on an exact retry. Duplicate operations converge without additional rooms, seats or state versions. A mismatched request, revoked credential or cross-room operation cannot claim the receipt. Migration `0003_magical_hawkeye.sql` was applied locally before dependent runtime changes.
- The browser saves an immutable, versioned, tab-scoped request before sending. Refresh restores an explicit retry screen; no POST retries automatically, and a cookie-only GET cannot erase the proof. Only a matching receipt or a confirmed fresh already-seated join acknowledges success. A clearly rejected first request returns the form for correction; uncertain and replayed attempts retain their proof. Failed storage writes prevent sending; failed deletion keeps the record visible. Invalid/future records are preserved for deliberate handling.
- Saved-kit recovery and entry attempts cannot silently discard each other's unresolved operation. Uncertain recovery blocks conflicting entry/navigation and retains its exact payload. A deliberate acknowledged abandonment provides an exit when another recovery invalidates that receipt; it does not undo any completed recovery. The original saved kit remains necessary for future recovery.
- Independent backend audit found a concurrent token-reuse classification issue; the fixed path rolls back and returns ENTRY_TOKEN_USED instead of a generic server error. Thirteen permanent entry cases cover accepted response loss, duplicate create/join, normalized/mismatched inputs, cookie precedence, started-game private projection, revocation, competing faction claims, failed receipt inserts and deterministic operation/token collision barriers. An independent eight-case isolated audit found no remaining confirmed custody/privacy defect.
- Ten client entry tests cover frozen payloads, strict parsing, fresh versus uncertain response acknowledgment, storage failures, retained proof through refresh and first-request rejection policy. Aggregate verification: **718/718 unit tests and 33/33 persisted/database tests pass**. TypeScript, repository lint and production build pass. Browser creation, refresh, same-seat rejoin, explicit retained-request retry, deliberate abandonment and corrected-invitation flows passed; mobile layout was inspected and improved. See `VISUAL_PLAYTEST.md` for precise scope.
- Richese source checks are recorded in `RICHESE_BLACK_MARKET_RULES.md` and `RICHESE_LEADER_AUDIT.md`. Black Market custody/payment/continuation need separate engine state; self-purchase and pre-bid Ixian exchange remain unresolved. Official indexed evidence did not establish a complete Richese leader roster/strengths or printed Auditor strength. No unverified component data was added.
- Full Advanced/expansion starts remain gated. Callers must publish the clone only after success; the public applyAction boundary preserves its input even if Harkonnen's random exchange fails. ## Printed player circles and authorized component inspection checkpoint

- Corrected the base storm's initial sector from 18 to the printed Meridian Storm Start (sector 1 in this board's numbering). The six printed player circles are fixed at sectors 2, 5, 8, 11, 14 and 17. They no longer redistribute with player count. After movement, order proceeds toward the next occupied circle counterclockwise; a circle already reached by the storm waits for the next circuit. Source: GF9 base rulebook pp.4,6–7, with printed map checked independently.
- New rooms persist playerPositions. Players can choose an unoccupied circle for themselves in the lobby; readiness resets, bot readiness remains automatic, and removal/join reuse vacancies without moving other circles. Public labels and six map markers show those positions. The first dialers bracket Storm Start using occupied circles rather than player-array endpoints. First dials remain 0–20; later Basic dials remain 1–3 from the last battle-wheel users, retaining previous storm dialers if there was no intervening battle.
- Existing rooms without positions derive the same six-circle defaults on read without mutation. Their next valid authoritative action persists the mapping and a one-time migration note. In-progress games retain their current storm, phase order and pending choices; the next completed storm uses the corrected order. Old lobbies display and adopt Storm Start 1. Past phases are not replayed.
- Eighteen focused regressions verify all eighteen sector boundaries, two through six players, sparse circles, ownership/range/occupied errors, readiness, bot removal/rejoin, first-dial ownership and secrecy, zero/full/multiple circuits, storm casualties/conservation, Weather Control, later dialers and legacy-state recovery. Three old endpoint expectations were updated to the corrected start. A persisted multiplayer race verifies competing circle choices, occupied retry rejection, owner enforcement and refresh without secret exposure.
- Expanded authorized inspection to setup traitor choices, private Face Dancers, Atreides auction faces, Ixian setup/pool/offered/purchased faces, winner card choices, public revealed battle components and private full-plan insight. Face Dancer guidance is distinct from traitor-call guidance. Action selection remains separate from enlargement; no opponent-hand or deck lookup was added. Setup now disables ordinary card activation until play, while retaining legal setup Karama responses and Truthtrance.
- The CHOAM/Richese rulebook p.4 expressly clarifies that the treachery discard pile is private and may only be searched under an enabling effect. A generic discard browser was therefore not added. Existing server projections keep the pile and deck identities private. Effect-specific inspection, including missing Nullentropy Box support, remains required.
- Independent UI review found clipped outer player circles and undefined CSS color variables; expanded board framing and corrected theme variables fix those issues. Browser verification covered saved circle selection/refresh, occupied-circle controls, setup inspection without selection, and enlarged revealed Stunner on desktop/mobile. See VISUAL_PLAYTEST.md for exact game checkpoints.

Complete Basic/tech-token AI games continue to pass after the order change. The earlier AI strength calibration is historical and has not been rerun for this revised seating model.

## Assassination and Sneak Attack checkpoint — 2026-09-06

Four Terror effects now have partial runtime, owner controls, private projection and AI support. Assassination immediately selects one random living ordinary native leader, updates its death count and battle-use marker, pays its native strength (Zoal 3), consumes the revealed token and resumes the interrupted entry. A client cannot choose the victim. Missing/unverified rosters, exceptional capture/Ghola custody and empty living pools reject before reveal or RNG. Advanced Harkonnen configurations uniformly reject this reveal so availability cannot disclose concealed captures.

Sneak Attack has a persisted reserve-count decision for zero through five available forces. Positive entry validates the recorded sector, storm and occupancy, transfers reserves without a spice fee or ordinary shipment/movement consumption, and resumes the original continuation. Owner-only limits and unfinished BG/Heighliners combinations are shown before revelation. Zero remains available after revealing; AI retains the hidden token when no positive entry is available. These restrictions identify unfinished implementation, not new official prohibitions.

Fifteen focused force/leader tests cover random selection, all five ordinary discs, Zoal, death history, invalid counts/sectors, resource conservation, reload and duplicate rejection, hidden projections and all four AI profiles including blocked offers. Independent review checked custody, RNG timing and continuation. Full integrated validation: **858 unit tests**, **45 persisted/API tests**, TypeScript, lint and production build pass. Source boundary: `MORITANI_ASSASSINATION_SNEAK.md`.

Atomics/Aftermath, Extortion, Enemy of My Enemy, advanced Assassinate Leaders, exceptional leader pools and combined arrival timing remain unfinished. Full Advanced and expansion starts stay disabled; these isolated QA scenarios do not constitute full-game certification.

## Enemy of My Enemy checkpoint — 2026-09-06

The special Terror alliance branch now has a persisted declaration, Karama response and entrant accept/refuse decision. Acceptance detaches both former partners, refunds only unused escrow attached to broken pairs, clears affected Nexus offers, forms the mutual alliance and returns the token hidden through the existing identity-rotation helper. Already paid shipment costs and committed forces stay unchanged. Refusal immediately enters the ordinary token effect within the same action; Moritani cannot decline again. Karama cancellation preserves the token and permits reveal/decline but prevents repeating that entry’s alliance offer.

Entrant UI branches before every owner-only token lookup. Public stages, names and alliance consequences contain no hidden face; owner controls show why an offer is unavailable. Unsupported mandatory effects and Ecaz are rejected before an offer, so the entrant is never asked to refuse into an unimplemented effect. All four AI profiles complete offer, response, reply, refusal and cancellation paths from private views. Sandtrout does not impose an invented ongoing special-alliance ban.

A related base-rule correction records `allySinceTurn` for Enemy and ordinary Nexus alliances. Newly allied co-occupants are not destroyed at the end of the same turn’s movement; the later mover still loses remaining co-occupants in a following turn if neither leaves. Future entry into an ally-occupied territory remains prohibited.

Fifteen focused alliance tests, independent source/code review, the full **873-unit** suite, **45 persisted/API** tests, typecheck, lint and build pass. An earlier run during test drafting failed only on the new public formation-turn field missing from a test expectation; the completed tests and integrated rerun pass. Browser acceptance survives refresh with hidden token return and exact shipment custody. Source/timing record: `MORITANI_ENEMY_ALLIANCE.md`.

The exact Karama-before-reply ordering is a tested draft using the existing power-response convention; a user clarification is pending because no specialized official timing ruling was retrieved. Full expansion starts remain gated. Atomics/Aftermath, Extortion, advanced Assassinate Leaders, Duke Vidal and other previously recorded scope remain unfinished.

## Duke Vidal shared-leader checkpoint — 2026-09-06

One separate Ecaz-identity disc of strength six now has explicit controller/source/turn custody. It is not appended to any native five-leader roster or traitor deck. Moritani's committed end-of-movement check counts distinct strongholds with actual upcoming battles, excludes Ecaz opponents and uses the existing storm/occupancy geometry; the Hidden Mobile Stronghold counts. A generic Karama window can prevent acquisition, with a once-per-turn marker preventing repeats. A held Duke is not stripped by a new cancellation.

The controller may select Duke in an ordinary supported battle. Death and six-strength bounty resolve on the shared disc; after one battle his controller clears while death history remains. An unused Moritani tenure expires at turn end, and a later qualifying turn can acquire him again. Ecaz permanent custody and unresolved allied-loan return are preserved by the custody helper; no loan-return destination is invented. The next storm clears the shared battle-use marker.

The public original purple/gold component and scrolling inspector show strength, custody, no-Traitor status and complete original-language core instructions. Controller-specific battle choices use explicit shared control, so an Ecaz identity never automatically grants control. Truthtrance battle questions and their readable logs now recognize Duke; traitor questions still reject him. Four integration regressions cover that distinction and legal promised plans.

Verification comprises nine pure custody tests, ten engine scenarios including Basic/Advanced casualties and all four AI candidate plans, four Duke/Truthtrance regressions, independent source/code review, full896-unit suite,45 persisted/API tests, typecheck, lint and build. Desktop browser QA exercises actual end-of-movement acquisition, full inspection, Duke battle use, set-aside status and refreshed second-battle options without Duke. These are bounded component/interaction checks, not complete expansion games.

Advanced Harkonnen configurations and captured/ghola custody remain explicitly unsupported for this acquisition/use slice because they affect the shared capture pool and release destination. Ecaz Ambassador acquisition, loans, Ecaz-only five-spice/Ghola revival and its five-in-Tanks revival threshold remain unfinished. Source details: `DUKE_VIDAL_RULES.md`. Advanced and expansion starts stay gated.

## Ecaz placement, automatic responses, pacing and rules checklists — 2026-09-06

Ecaz now has an eleven-token Ambassador inventory, escalating end-of-Revival placement, owner controls, public compact/expanded inspectors and all-four-profile placement choices. The pure custody model covers original-group recycling, reusable Ecaz, permanent BG removal and destruction return. Runtime entry effects and destruction hooks are still missing. Public unplaced supply is a documented default-open inference; CHOAM market → deferred technology income → Ecaz placement is a composed implementation order, not certified combined FAQ priority. Full Ecaz setup, Occupy, Duke revival, optional modules and expansion starts remain unfinished/gated.

A sole legal Advanced battle casualty allocation now settles immediately, while real typed-casualty, Ixian substitution and card-retention choices remain. Power responses automatically proceed when the owner and all players without a usable physical cancellation card are the only seats left. Shared physical eligibility checks cover Advanced BG conversion, sealed/prescience bindings and Moritani retention reservations. Explicit passes and cancel-card IDs are viewer-local; automatic eligibility is not published per seat. Active Truthtrance/phase opening retains precedence, restored nested windows recheck current custody, and voluntary battle promises are validated before automatic consequences. Promise-constrained physical candidates remain conservative until commit, so this is not yet complete promise-aware option filtering.

Successful casualties, Ecaz placements, Emperor auction income and Harkonnen bonus draws emit server-authored public notice metadata. A local toggle controls a nonblocking, reduced-motion-aware queue with 1.5 seconds per event, consumed disabled events and an initial-history baseline. A two-effect auction browser check showed the Emperor notice followed by Harkonnen's bonus, while bidding had already continued; no drawn identity enters public event text. Untagged powers, acknowledgement-only auction payment/full-plan/collection flows and further animations still require work.

Online AI now commits at most one accepted decision per 1.5-second persisted deadline, including consented human autopilot. Human actions commit immediately. Authenticated reads resume pending work; overlapping reads share one per-room worker within an isolate, with database CAS still fencing separate workers and take-back-control. Legacy uncancelable responses use a separate bounded authoritative continuation, keeping `viewGame` pure. Offline simulations remain fast. Durable progress while every client stays disconnected is still a liveness gap after the bounded request worker ends. See AI_PACING.md and AUTOMATIC_RESPONSE_AUDIT.md.

The in-app reference now includes five-facet feature checklists for the reviewed Ecaz, automatic-action and pacing slices, with internal cross-links and actual regression evidence. This is a growing traceable checklist, not finished coverage for every rule. Eighteen of the existing fifty ordinary leader identities have original portraits: full Atreides, Harkonnen and Emperor rosters plus the earlier Fremen/Guild/BG pilot faces. CHOAM/Richese component verification, remaining portraits and complete component browser acceptance remain open.

Integrated verification: **991/991 rules/client tests and 63/63 persisted/API tests pass**, with typecheck, lint and production build passing. The initial integration identified obsolete mandatory-pass expectations; these were changed to immediate-resolution assertions or genuine pre-held cancellation resources while preserving privacy, custody, refresh and concurrency checks. Fifteen focused automatic-response cases and seven persistence/coalescing cases are included in those totals. Independent reviews covered Ecaz authority/interpretation boundaries, server pacing and automatic continuation. Browser evidence is in VISUAL_PLAYTEST.md; this does not certify complete Advanced/expansion games.

## Ambassador entry and automatic decisions checkpoint (2026-09-06)

Implemented five Ambassador effects end to end: Emperor bank grant, Atreides current-hand snapshot, Harkonnen random held Traitor snapshot (including physical Tleilaxu Face Dancers), CHOAM chosen card sales, and Ixian discard/replacement. Bene Gesserit can copy those supported effects from outside the original six-token supply, regardless current custody zones. Ecaz chooses whether to trigger and its current allied beneficiary; the beneficiary controls its own private choices. Every action binds the persisted event ID and stage. Inspection results remain private snapshots and require no acknowledgement.

The committed entry pauses subsequent work without replaying movement or shipment. Self, ally, advisors and physical-marker faction immunity are applied independently of a copied effect. Cohort replenishment waits until the final triggered effect finishes; BG never re-enters the pool. Exposed-city storm crossings and actual Lasgun–shield explosions return Ambassadors to supply without triggering them. Traitor precedence preserves a token when it prevents an explosion.

Independent review found and fixed two exchange deadlocks: CHOAM cash-in and delayed Truthtrance disposal could otherwise spend the beneficiary’s final required discard. Voluntary interruptions must preserve a legal completion, including queued Truthtrance reservations. Legal interruptions retaining a card still complete through JSON recovery.

Auction payments now execute automatically only when the exact declared own/ally split is funded and no physically usable Karama remains. Genuine payment choices and provisional unfunded recovery are retained. Full-plan inspection has no Finish action; the persistent private inspector also supports an external Atreides ally. Authenticated recovery schedules both legacy decision types and persists only actual changes through the existing CAS fence.

Verification: 1,029/1,029 rules/client tests; 74/74 persisted/API tests; TypeScript, lint and production build pass. Logs: `/tmp/dune-ambassador-full.log`, `/tmp/dune-ambassador-multiplayer.log`, `/tmp/dune-ambassador-type.log`, `/tmp/dune-ambassador-lint.log`, `/tmp/dune-ambassador-build.log`. The fresh 120-game Basic calibration completed with no deadlock, action-limit, exception or invariant failure; 47,965 accepted actions, 9,081 rejected candidate actions. Relative to the prior same-seed run, every per-game field except accepted-action count and elapsed time is unchanged.

Limits: Ecaz/Duke/alliance, Fremen movement, Guild shipment, Richese purchase and Tleilaxu revival effects still need completion; competing arrival ordering remains unresolved and rejects the original entry atomically. Occupy, full Ecaz setup, other expansion systems and full-mode games remain unfinished. The first-slice checklist in the internal rules marks these boundaries. No expansion or Advanced start gate was removed, and the user-removed automation stays removed.

Four additional original Fremen portraits complete the five native Fremen faces: Chani6, Otheym5, Shadout Mapes3 and Jamis2 join Stilgar7.
